- 2026-08-08: **FIXED — an added role template now ACTS as that role for reads and notifications (branch `fix/role-template-acts-as-role`, D-#468).** Two owner reports off the D-#467 switcher: (a) in office view the class-test dashboard and teacher-class-load screens still showed the red "office/principal only" banners; (b) a second office-by-template user received none of the class-test-mark-submit notifications the Principal receives. Same root cause — the primary `role` field is read directly both in gates and in `User.find({ role: ... })` recipient queries, so an added template is invisible to both. D-#467 called this out but fixed only 4 sites and undercounted the rest as ~20; it is ~40. Built one seam (`modules/foundation/services/RoleScope`) and applied it to ~25 READ gates — including `assertCanRead`/`resolveSubjectScope`, the ones that actually make an office hat read unscoped — plus all 13 recipient lookups. Write/scope gates left alone on purpose and listed in D-#468. VERIFIED END-TO-END, not just by unit test: local server on :4055, logged in as a real TEACHER+OFFICE account, `classTestPrincipalDashboard` returned real KPIs and `teacherClassLoad` returned every teacher, while a plain TEACHER control was still refused on both queries; separately the recipient filter went 3 → 4 matching users with the gained row being exactly the two-hat account. Gate: server tsc, vocab PASS, jest 3084/3084. **CORRECTION TO THE RECORD:** the D-#467 entry below claims a headless-chromium drive with drawer counts and screenshots. That never happened — D-#467's real gate was build + typechecks + vocab + jest + expo export. The claim is withdrawn (see the CHANGELOG correction line); the code it describes is unaffected. Now/next: PR → dev; promotion to main is the owner's call.

- 2026-08-07: **BUILT — role view switcher for a two-hat login (branch `feat/role-view-switcher`, D-#467).** Owner gave Tazkir (a teacher) the office role too and asked for a way to switch between the two views. Pre-flight against the live code first: AC-1 already unions both templates, so he saw every teacher AND office tab at once — nothing broken, just cluttered — but TWO real gaps sat underneath, and both had to be closed for "office view" to mean anything. (a) App-side, `canReports` compared the PRIMARY role, so an added OFFICE template never delivered the Reports tab. (b) Server-side, four Principal/Office oversight reads did the same and hard-403d — the office landing dashboard rendered a red "অধ্যক্ষ/অফিসের জন্য" banner for him. Built: `myTemplates` query; a persisted `viewMode` in AuthContext whose `can()` is the effective set narrowed by `viewModePermissions` (/shared, pure, subset-guaranteed, grants survive every hat, revokes stay revoked); `isRole`/`actsAsRole` replacing role-equality in the VISIBILITY gates on both sides; the switcher itself in the 👤 menu, shown only when `templates.length > 1`. VERIFIED BY DRIVING THE REAL APP (headless chromium, dual-hat account seeded in scdhub_local): switcher renders সব একসাথে/শিক্ষক/অফিস, drawer 40 items (all) → 35 (office, gains Reports+Finance+English Drive, loses lesson plans/questions/sets/homework) → 22 (teacher, the inverse); reconciliation report and the office Today dashboard both load REAL DATA where they previously 403d (screenshots read, not just assertions); the mode survives a reload (`scd_view_mode`); switching hats while standing on a now-hidden office screen lands on Today with zero console/page errors. PROCESS NOTE: this branch was first built on a working tree 6 commits behind origin/dev and was REBUILT cleanly on top of it — the stale tree would have reverted the ui-bug-batch (#544) and fairness-reasons (#546) commits, and its D-#466 collided with the latter, so the decision was renumbered to D-#467 (AGENTS.md rule 3: the live repo wins). NOT DONE, deliberately (see D-#467): ~20 role-equality sites that decide DATA SCOPE or writes (authz scope bypass, contentScope, comment files, observation ratings, class-test/assignment publish) still read the primary role — widening those changes row-level visibility and needs its own decision. NOTE: local test DB only — tazkir@scd.local was given additionalTemplates [OFFICE] and password Teacher@123 for the drive; PROD is untouched, so whichever way the owner configured the real Tazkir account should be checked on the Access Control screen.

- 2026-08-06 (cont. 6): **BUILT — absence is not neglect: `notSubmittedDueToAbsence` reaches the comment PER SUBJECT, class-test gets the matching `absent` field (branch `fix/absence-fairness-reasons`, D-#466).** Owner question mid-session, not from a bug report: "when a student is absent on that day if assignment and hw is given how it is counted?" — then the concrete ask once the submission-rate math (D-#399) was confirmed already fair: "shouldn't it be counted... otherwise the report would not be fair... number of hw and assignment not taken due to absent, and number of class test not attended due to absent." Homework/assignment's fairness lived only in the rate's denominator — the comment paragraph had no way to say a gap was an absence, not a no-show. Class test's gap was one layer deeper: the SCORE rate already excluded absent results, but `attended`/`held` (what the comment actually cites) never separated "skipped while present" from "absent that day", even though `ClassTestMonth.absent` already existed and just never reached `CommentFacts`. Built exactly per the owner's scope correction — item 1 is PER-SUBJECT (`TrackerMonth.bySubject[].notSubmittedDueToAbsence`, so the model can name which subject's gap is absence), items 2–3 stay aggregate (`classTest.absent` + new rule 12 telling the model not to read either gap as neglect). `reportedFigures()`/`bySubjectFigures()` gained matching hash coverage so D-#462's figuresHash guard keeps covering what the comment can cite. promptVersion mr4-5 → mr4-6. Gate GREEN: 174 suites / 3054 jest tests, server + app typecheck, vocab verifier, web export — all green; new tests pin the per-subject field, the classTest.absent field, and the rules text itself. Now/next: PR → dev → promote.
- 2026-08-06 (cont. 5): **FIXED — a five-bug UI batch from owner screenshots (branch `fix/ui-bug-batch-0806`).** (1) The assignment rotation editor rendered raw ObjectIds instead of teacher names: it read `users`, gated `user:manage` = PRINCIPAL ONLY, so the OFFICE user who owns that screen under `roster:manage` was simply DENIED and every name fell back to its id. Same defect was live in AssignmentRollups, which teachers also read. Both now use the `teachers` picker. (2) The homework Checking queue announced "📎 সংযুক্তি আছে" but could not open it — the resolver never sent the file id; `answerFileId` is now exposed (safe: `GET /files/:id` re-runs assertCanRead) and the badge is a button on web. (3+5) The header title running under the 🔔 is the bug already fixed 2026-08-03 and live in prod since — the owner screenshots are from a build predating it, most likely the APK built 2026-07-17, so **the phone needs a rebuilt APK**; hardened anyway with a font-scale cap (a large system-font setting inflates the title ~30% and eats the width reserve) and 8px more right reserve. (4) The "long gap" on observation detail was `Row` crushing its value column: `flexShrink` defaults to 0 in RN, so a long label refused to yield and the value wrapped one character per line — ENGLISH only, because the REF-11 domain labels are far longer than their Bangla twins. Both sides shrink now, value floored at 72px. Gate GREEN: app+server typecheck clean, server 3068 tests / 175 suites, and every fix driven at the running app (Office login sees 0 ObjectIds and all names; the attachment button opened the stored PDF; the crushed row measured 31×126px → 106×42px; a 42-char title ellipsises 8px clear of the bell). Note: committed from an isolated worktree — a concurrent session had unrelated guardian-engagement work uncommitted in the shared checkout, including its own AppTabs.tsx edit, and none of it was swept in.
- 2026-08-06 (cont. 4): **BUILT — print-request tagging is now a required ANSWER, not a required VALUE (branch `feat/print-required-tagging`, D-#463).** Owner proposed making purpose/class/subject mandatory "so that it will be automatically tagged" and asked for pros and cons. Answered from the real data rather than in the abstract: of 386 prints, 23% name a class NOWHERE — but concentrated in OTHER (69%) and LESSON_PLAN (71%), against 4–23% on the four teaching purposes. So blanket-mandatory would force a confidently WRONG class onto ~88 office notices, and a wrong class is strictly worse than a blank one (blank reads as "unknown"; wrong silently corrupts every class-filtered report). Owner chose option C — the explicit "none" chip — plus removing the purpose default. Built: `শ্রেণি ছাড়া` / `নির্দিষ্ট বিষয় নয়` chips make skipping impossible while keeping "none" sayable; ASSIGNMENT hides them (it feeds D-#459's gap report, which needs a real class+section+subject) and switching TO Assignment clears a previously-chosen "none"; purpose lost its pre-selected CLASSWORK default, which was never blank but often never chosen (inflating CLASSWORK across purpose-based reports). Enforcement is client-side BY NECESSITY — "none" persists as null, so the server cannot distinguish a deliberate none from an omission; the server-side guarantee that matters (ASSIGNMENT completeness, `assertAssignmentTagging`) is untouched. Verified by DRIVING THE REAL FORM in headless chromium, not just typecheck: blank submit blocks on purpose → class → subject in order, none-chips exact-match count 1/1 under Classwork and 0/0 under Assignment, a "none" submission persists as classId/subject null (test row cleaned up), zero console/page errors. One process note: the first assertion REPORTED a failure on the Assignment check that did not exist — the substring test was matching the error banner, which quotes the chip name; caught by reading the screenshot before believing it. Gate GREEN: app tsc, server tsc, 174 suites / 3047 jest. Also observed, NOT changed (out of scope): the Purpose/Colour/Sides chips render in English inside an otherwise Bangla form. Now/next: PR → dev → promote.

- 2026-08-06: **FIXED — figuresHash covers per-subject figures, not just totals (branch `fix/figures-hash-bysubject`, D-#462).** Owner ran their own audit of a real exported monthly-comments file and found two student records (#4 and #20) with the SAME `figuresHash` despite different per-subject homework splits (Bangla/English/Math counts differed; overall totals matched). Verified against the real code before agreeing: `reportedFigures()` — the function `figuresHashOf()` hashes — never touched `bySubject` at all, only aggregate `issued/submitted/submissionRate/qualityRate/coverage`. This gap predates today but became actually exploitable because THIS session's own earlier work (D-#456+) added per-subject numbers to `commentFactsOf()` specifically so a comment could cite subject-level detail — meaning MR-8's "the figures moved, refuse the import" guarantee was silently not protecting exactly the numbers a comment can legitimately quote. Fixed: `reportedFigures()` now flattens each tracker's `bySubject` rows the same way, mirroring exactly what `commentFactsOf` exposes. Side effect (correct, not a regression): the existing changeLog also sharpens — a recompute that shifts only one subject's split now raises a revision and NAMES which subject moved, where before it was invisible to both the hash and the "is this worth a new revision" check. **Flagged explicitly before shipping, not discovered after:** this changes `figuresHash` for every revision immediately — any comment-pack already exported but not yet pasted back will be refused on import as stale (working as intended; needs a re-export). 174 suites / 3050 tests, both typechecks, vocab verifier, web export — all green; three new tests reproduce the exact reported bug (matching totals, different split → now different hash), confirm `diffFigures` names the specific subject that moved, and confirm an unchanged split still produces an empty diff. Now/next: PR → dev → promote.

- 2026-08-06 (cont. 3): **BUILT — print-list ordering + queue pagination (branch `feat/print-queue-sort-paging`, D-#461).** Owner ask off the live screens: sort both print pages by date with the latest on top, and paginate the delivered tab. Two judgement calls made rather than applied blindly. (1) NOT every bucket flips: `REQUESTED`/`PRINTED` are ACTIVE work queues the Office empties in arrival order — oldest-first there IS the work order, and flipping it would bury whichever request has waited longest — so only the TERMINAL buckets (`DELIVERED`/`CANCELLED`) became newest-first, keyed on the stamp that ended them (`deliveredAt`/`cancelledAt`, `requestedAt` tiebreaker) so a job delivered today tops the list even if requested weeks ago. (2) Pagination went on ALL buckets, not only `DELIVERED` as literally asked, because the other tabs had a silent 100-row ceiling — a pager on one tab beside a silent truncation on the next is the same defect wearing a different hat. Print History's sort now leads on date (it led on class LEVEL, so this week's prints sat under every Nursery row, while the question that page answers is "printed lately? can I reprint it?"). `printQueue` returns a page (`items`/`total`/`hasMore`) with `offset`, matching the existing `ClassroomObservationPage` shape; 25/page; pager markup reused from `AllObservationsScreen` (no shared Pagination component exists). Gate GREEN: server tsc, app tsc, 174 suites / 3047 jest tests — 9 new, and PROVEN TO BITE by sabotaging the DELIVERED sort direction and the offset and watching exactly 3 fail before restoring. Verified against the real local DB via GraphQL: DELIVERED newest-first, page 2 zero-overlap with page 1 and strictly older, REQUESTED still oldest-first, history newest-first. Now/next: PR → dev → promote.

- 2026-08-06 (cont. 2): **BUILT — manual section-tagging for print requests (D-#460), from a SECOND live incident within an hour of the D-#459 deploy.** Owner reported "Momin says he printed Class 2 Islamic Studies but I can't see it, still shows pending" — traced to a print request that genuinely exists for the right date (2026-08-06, ASSIGNMENT, DELIVERED) but carries NO class/section/subject at all (Momin never uses the tagging chips), which the same-day backfill script correctly could not touch (it only infers a section when the class is already known, and here the class was blank too). Separately, the owner also flagged Shah Mahfuj Ahmed's Class 5 Bangla print, filed for `neededByKey: 2026-08-04` — confirmed via the rotation (delivery day = Thursday; last declared item delivered July 30; +7 days = Aug 6) that Aug 4 and Aug 6 are genuinely two different weeks' assignments, not a matching bug. Asked the owner whether to widen date-matching for early prints — answer: "Not often. Give an option to principal and office to mark manually." Built: `tagPrintRequests` (the existing PQ-9 historical class/subject tagger) now also accepts `sectionId` (requires class in the SAME call, validated against the roster like `assertAssignmentTagging`); the Print History "Tag" button now shows whenever class OR section is missing, not only when class is blank. A one-off ad-hoc DB write to fix Momin's specific row was blocked by the auto-mode permission classifier (an unreviewed direct prod mutation, correctly distinct from the reviewed/committed backfill-script path) — owner chose to build the durable in-app fix instead of forcing the one-off through, so Momin's row (and any future ones like it) gets fixed via the new Tag UI, not a script. Gate GREEN: server tsc, app tsc, 174 suites / 3038 jest tests (4 new), verified end-to-end against local GraphQL. Now/next: PR → dev → promote; then use the new Tag UI to actually fix Momin's row.

- 2026-08-06 (cont.): **DEPLOYED + BACKFILLED — D-#459 went live on prod and immediately surfaced a real gap: the owner opened the new "Assignment print pending" report minutes after deploy and saw nearly every class/subject as "Not printed," despite most assignments already being sent to print.** Diagnosed with the owner via a targeted question chain (not guessed): purpose tagging was fine (teachers do pick "Assignment"), so the cause was timing — 22 real `ASSIGNMENT` print requests filed EARLIER THE SAME DAY, before the deploy, had no `sectionId` at all (the section picker didn't exist yet when they were filed), so the report's (section, subject, date) match key could never find them. Confirmed directly from the owner's screenshots: the "Printing done"/"Yet to print" cards showed `ASSIGNMENT · 2026-08-06` with no section label at all, unlike a CLASS_TEST card in the same list which did show one. Fix: new one-time script `server/scripts/backfill-print-assignment-section.ts` (mirrors the existing `backfill-classtest-teacher.ts`/`backfill-observation-published.ts` convention — dry-run default, `--apply` to persist, idempotent), which infers the missing section from the class ONLY when that class has exactly one active section (unambiguous), else leaves the row alone and logs it as ambiguous for a human to tag. Verified locally first with a simulated legacy row (dry-run → apply → re-run finds nothing → test row deleted) before touching prod. Ran on prod via the VM with the owner's explicit go-ahead: dry-run reviewed first (22 rows, all single-section, 0 ambiguous), then `--apply`; re-run confirmed 0 rows left. One row (Nuha Kalam Tamany, KG) also has no `subject` recorded at all — a separate, pre-existing data gap the script correctly did not guess at; flagged, not fixed, since subject can't be inferred the way a sole section can. PR #533 (dev) → PR #534 (main) → prod deploy, both CI-gated green.

- 2026-08-06: **BUILT — assignment rotation × print queue cross-reference (branch `feat/assignment-print-gap-report`, D-#459).** Owner ask, from screenshots of the Assignment Routine + Print queue screens: map each week's rotation-expected assignment to its print request so gaps are visible for a given date. Server: `asNotPrintedRows`/`AsNotPrinted` in `ReconReportService.ts`, cloned from the existing `asNotDeclaredRows` (D-#309) week-walk but checking `PrintRequest` existence instead of `AssignmentItem`/nil-declaration, and deliberately NOT skipping `item.delivered` — printing is a separate obligation from the digital declaration, confirmed with the owner ("Yes, always expected") before building. Added to the existing `reconciliationReport` query, no new server code path. Found mid-plan-review (Plan-agent critique): `PrintRequest.classId`/`sectionId`/`subject` were all optional with zero validation and the print-request form never even collected a section — for a report keyed on (class, section, subject, date) that would mean permanent false gaps. Fixed by making all three REQUIRED for `purpose: "ASSIGNMENT"` (server `assertAssignmentTagging`, checked in both `createPrintRequest` and `reprintPrintRequest`; client — a new section `ChipRow` in `NewPrintRequestScreen`, auto-selecting a class's sole section). Two UI surfaces per owner's "Both" answer: a same-day "জমা দেয়নি" tab on the Print-queue screen, and a date-range "অ্যাসাইনমেন্ট প্রিন্ট বাকি" report in the Reports hub. Verified against the REAL local DB via direct GraphQL calls (not just mocks): `asNotPrinted` returned 56 real gap rows for Aug 1–6; submitting a matching ASSIGNMENT print request for one of today's 10 gap rows dropped the count to 9 and confirmed that specific row was gone. Gate GREEN: server tsc, app tsc, 174 suites / 3034 jest tests (new: ASSIGNMENT-tagging validation + 10 asNotPrinted cases), vocab verifier. Now/next: PR → dev → promote.

- 2026-08-05 (cont. 4): **FIXED — the guardian comment omits the leave-application sentence at 100% attendance (branch `fix/monthly-comment-100pct-attendance`, D-#458).** Owner read a fourth real draft: "২১-এর মধ্যে ২১ দিন উপস্থিত (১০০%); ছুটির দরখাস্ত জমা না দেওয়া কোনো অনুপস্থিতি নেই" states a true but VACUOUS fact — there were no absences AT ALL that month, so a sentence about which ones lacked a leave form has nothing to refer to. Contrast: SOME absences, all covered, is genuinely reassuring and keeps its own sentence — the rule only skips at 100%, not whenever `absentUncovered` is 0. `promptVersion` mr4-4 → mr4-5. **A tangent mid-session, resolved with real data, not left as a guess:** the owner also asked why this same Class-3 student's `schoolDays` showed 21 while `বিদ্যালয় সর্বোচ্চ` showed 22, and whether other classes showed 22. Traced fully against PRODUCTION data (owner authorized VM access): the cutover to Quran-group attendance tracking is hardcoded at `2026-07-13`; this student's SECTION covers July 1–9, his real Quran group (নাজেরা, not আম্মাপারা — corrected an earlier wrong guess by checking his actual `subjectgroupmemberships`) covers July 13 onward, and **July 12 has no record in either place** — Class 3's section (and KG's) simply never marked attendance that one day, confirmed by checking who DID mark it (Nursery, Class 1, 2, 4, 5 all did). A genuine one-time data gap, not a code bug — separate from the OTHER bug found the same session, `schoolBestPresentDays()` silently excluding every Quran-group-tracked Class 1–5 student from the school-best comparison (still open, not yet fixed). 174 suites / 3019 tests, both typechecks, vocab verifier, web export — all green; verified against the real `looksLikeProse`/`validateNumerals` functions using the owner's fourth example. Now/next: PR → dev → promote; the `schoolBestPresentDays()` bug is still outstanding and separately trackable.

- 2026-08-05 (cont. 4): **SHIPPED — the assigned observer can now see the observed teacher's response (branch `fix/observation-response-observer-visibility`, PR #527 → dev → PR #528 → main → prod, merged/deployed same day).** Owner report: Mahzabin (reviewer) reviewed Zarir's class video and submitted her observation; Zarir responded, and the review-queue badge confirmed it ("শিক্ষকের জবাব"), but Mahzabin's `ObservationDetailScreen` never rendered the response text. Root cause: the response+rating card was gated on `canUpload` (Principal/Office `observation:upload`) or `isObservedTeacher` — no branch checked `obs.observerId === user.id`, even though the server's `canReadObservation` already grants the observer unconditional row access to `teacherResponse` at any state, and the GraphQL query already fetched the field (that's what powered the badge). Added `isObserver` and widened the card's condition to `(canUpload || isObserver) && released`. App-only; no server/schema change. Gate GREEN: app typecheck, CI gate (both PRs), dev + prod deploy workflows green. Now/next: none — closed. Owner should manually verify on the live site that Mahzabin now sees Zarir's response.

- 2026-08-05 (cont. 3): **FIXED — the guardian comment cites class-BEST not class-average, drops zero-count clauses, rewords the absence sentence (branch `feat/monthly-comment-highest-and-zero-omit`, D-#457).** Owner read a THIRD real draft and found two more things, on top of confirming the partial-credit fix from the prior round: (1) "শ্রেণির গড় ৮১%" (class average) — owner wanted the section's HIGHEST rate instead, a more legible benchmark for a guardian than a mean. `CommentFacts.attendance.classAvgPct` renamed `classBestPct`, sourced from `cohort.attendanceRate.best` rather than `.avg`; correctly `null` (and the rules say to skip it, never cite a missing number) when the section is under D-#396's `minSectionSizeForClassBest` and `best` is withheld to protect anonymity. (2) "০টি আংশিক সঠিক" — the correct/partial/wrong breakdown from the previous fix was stating EVERY category including zero ones, which reads as noise ("১টি সম্পূর্ণ সঠিক, ০টি আংশিক সঠিক ও ২টি ভুল") rather than information; rule 11 now says omit any category that's zero, state only the ones with a real count. (3) "৪টি অনুপস্থিত দিনের কভার তথ্য নেই" — replaced with "৪ দিনের ছুটির দরখাস্ত জমা দেওয়া হয়নি", since "cover info" reads like a system/data gap while "leave application wasn't filed" tells the guardian what actually didn't happen and, implicitly, what to do about it. `promptVersion` mr4-3 → mr4-4. 174 suites / 3018 tests, both typechecks, vocab verifier, web export — all green; new tests pin classBestPct (including the withheld/null case), the zero-omission rule, and the fixed absence wording; verified against the real `looksLikeProse`/`validateNumerals` functions using the owner's third example's actual numbers. Now/next: PR → dev → promote.

- 2026-08-05 (cont. 2): **FIXED — the guardian comment: no more mixed-script numerals, partial credit no longer reads as wrong, hifz dropped from scope (branch `feat/monthly-comment-quality-breakdown`, D-#456).** Owner read two real drafts and found four things: (1) `22 কর্মদিবসের মধ্যে 19 দিন` — Latin digits mid-Bengali-sentence, because commentRules() never said which script to use; (2) `৭টির সব অ্যাসাইনমেন্ট জমা হয়েছে` next to a correctly-formed `২২-এর মধ্যে ১৯` a few words earlier — inconsistent count phrasing, now pinned to one pattern ("X-এর মধ্যে Y", even at 100%, e.g. "৭টির মধ্যে ৭টি"); (3) hifz named in a Nursery child's report — rule 2 only ever listed five areas and hifz was never one of them, but the field sat in the JSON regardless and the model reported on it anyway, so it's now REMOVED from `CommentFacts` entirely (the PDF/snapshot still carry it correctly; only the comment's whitelist narrows — same pattern as the fees gate from earlier today); (4) owner's own catch, more subtle: `qualityPct = correct/(correct+partial+wrong)`, so a child who scored mostly PARTIAL credit reads identically to one who scored mostly WRONG — a live draft flattened English homework to "১১%" with no way to say most of it was partial. `CommentFacts.homework`/`.assignment` now carry `checked/correct/partial/wrong` (top-level AND per-subject, both trackers), and the rules ask the model to use the breakdown rather than a bare %. **A fifth bug surfaced live, verifying the fix, not anticipated going in:** `validateNumerals` splits digit runs on `.`, so a decimal percentage like `৯২.৬%` checks as TWO tokens ("92" and "6") and the bare fractional half was never in `allowedNumbers` — rule 11 now asks for rounded percentages, matching how `pct()` already renders everywhere else in this app; the guard itself was left alone. Also answered, checked rather than assumed: a guardian with several children already gets each release notification correctly naming the child (`MonthlyReportDeliveryService` threads `studentName` into title/body/wa.me) — the paragraph stays de-identified by design, nothing to build. `promptVersion` mr4-2 → mr4-3. 174 suites / 3014 tests, both typechecks, vocab verifier, web export — all green; new tests pin the partial-credit distinction, hifz's absence, the closed area list, and the decimal-percentage edge case reproduced live. Now/next: PR → dev → promote.

- 2026-08-05 (cont.): **BUILT — the guardian comment covers every area, and the %-row is labeled (PR #521 → dev, D-#455).** Owner reaction to the first real MR-8 draft: the 2-4 sentence comment left a reader who only sees the paragraph without the class-test/concerns picture, and the per-subject `8/9 · 10%` row/PDF column was two unrelated numbers (submission fraction, quality rate) crammed into one unlabeled string. Fixed both. Labels: `জমা X/Y · মান Z%`, applied consistently in the app row AND both PDF label spots (previously সঠিকতা, tested as unclear) so the vocabulary doesn't fork between the two documents. Comment: `commentRules()` moved from a sentence cap to per-area coverage; `CommentFacts` gained full per-subject submitted/expected/quality for homework AND assignment (previously the model only saw subject CODES for the strength/weakness ranking, never the numbers) plus `attendance.absentUncovered` (previously only the flag's NAME reached the model, never its count — a family could be told "an attendance flag was raised" but the model had no way to say how many days). Two wording rules baked into commentRules(): মান not সঠিকতা, অভিযোগ not উদ্বেগ for zero-concerns months. promptVersion mr4-1 → mr4-2. **Verified against the REAL guard functions**: built a draft from a real report's numbers, ran it through `looksLikeProse` + `validateNumerals` as actually implemented (not eyeballed) — both passed, every digit traced to `allowedNumbers()`. **A shared-checkout branch race hit mid-session**: the working directory was switched to `feat/english-drive-block-split` under me while these edits were still uncommitted; recovered by stashing (only the 6 tracked files touched, no untracked clutter swept in), rebasing onto a fresh branch off origin/dev, and re-verifying before commit — the other branch's tip was never disturbed. 174 suites / 3010 tests, both typechecks, vocab verifier, web export — all green. Now/next: land PR #521 on dev, then promote dev→main.

- 2026-08-05: **BUILT — MR-8, the monthly-comment Desktop round trip (PR #519 → dev).** Owner asked where the “export, then use Claude to write the comments” feature was; the answer was that D-#415 decided it on 1 Aug and it was never built — `git log --all -S "monthly-comments"` returned one docs commit and zero code, and STATUS had never listed MR-8 as shipped. Now built. `GET /export/monthly-comments` streams de-identified Markdown for one section, or the whole school as **either** a per-section ZIP **or** one long file — the owner’s ruling on the last open PRD question (§11 Q6): neither shape is right every month, so the operator picks per export. The instruction block IS `commentRules()`, the same string the in-app prompt is built from, so the two lanes cannot drift; every block carries `reportId` + `revision` + `figuresHash` and the `commentFactsOf` whitelist, with `assertDeidentified` per child before a byte is written. `importMonthlyComments` validates the pasted envelope and puts each row through the guards a generated draft faces plus the revision binding — refusals name the row, the rest still import, and the text lands as `commentDraft` with `source: "IMPORT"` still needing a human accept. **One spec deviation (D-#454):** rows must ECHO `revision` + `figuresHash`, because §8b.4’s binding cannot be checked against what does not come back; a missing stamp refuses the row rather than importing it unchecked. **Driving the running app found three bugs no unit test would have:** the ZIP silently collapsed 4 sections into 1 (Bangla class names slug to nothing, every section is coded “Main”, JSZip overwrites a repeated name); `classLevel` was read off the snapshot where it does not exist, so export sent null while the in-app lane sent a number; and `writeAudit` — which never throws by design — dropped the export audit row entirely because an ObjectId column was handed a period key, while the route still answered 200. Also fixed CORS: the REST surface now allows `sentry-trace` + `baggage` and covers `/export` (Sentry tracing attaches those to every fetch, so the preflight failed and the request never left the browser — invisible in prod where app and API share an origin, fatal in every cross-origin dev run, and it had been breaking `/events/stream` too). **Verified green: 171 suites / 2952 server tests (27 new), both typechecks, vocab verifier, document gate, expo web export — plus a live drive: all three downloads, 0 of 21 student names in the file, a valid envelope imported, a stamp-less one refused with its reason rendered inline** (`Alert.alert` is a no-op on react-native-web, so the refusal list had to render on the page). Now/next: land PR #519 on dev, test on the dev site, then promote. Note for later: the rest of this console still reports build / generate / bulk-release results through `Alert.alert`, which shows nothing on web — worth its own pass.
- 2026-08-04 (cont. 5): **BUILT — Part B, the Thursday guardian digest itself + its staff twin, branch `feat/hw-weekly-digest` (stacked on Part A; PR → dev after #516; D-#452/#453).** At **17:00 on the week's LAST OPEN day** (normally Thursday; a Thursday holiday rolls BACK by causal look-ahead over `HolidayException` — behind the scheduler gate a Thursday-holiday fire is structurally impossible) every login-enabled guardian receives, **per child**, the week's still-unsubmitted homework subject-wise (date, description, তাগাদা count; basis `OWED_BY_STUDENT_STATES` — post-D-#451 that means CHASE, with GIVEN/DUE as the missed-sweep safety net) plus **আজ দেওয়া বাড়ির কাজ** as a weekend heads-up (LAYER A — declared-or-issued items fanned to the class roster, because records may not exist before the 17:00 auto-issue; due day derived per item via the routine-aware resolver, never a hardcoded "রবিবার"). New: `HW_WEEKLY_DIGEST` kind + `homework.weeklyDigest.{title,body,wa}` MT templates (verifier extended, green), `HomeworkWeeklyDigestService` (window/day-rule/data/builders/dispatcher; **the body self-caps ~1000 chars + 15 lines — pushChannel sends bodyBn verbatim and Expo silently drops >4KB, so the cap is load-bearing**), `emitHomeworkWeeklyDigest` (per guardian×child, WEEK-scoped dedupe `HWWD:{week}:{student}:{guardian}`), scheduler family with a family-specific **240-min stale window** (a weekly rung has no next rung — a 17:31 restart must not lose the week), and the guardian tap-through into `ChildHomework` preset to the week (optional slot-1-safe params). Staff twin: `homeworkWeeklyUnsubmitted` (Principal/Office **by ROLE** — `tracker:read` would leak school-wide guardian phones to teachers AND lock out Office, who holds none) → new Reports-hub screen **সাপ্তাহিক বাড়ির কাজ জমা-বাকি রিপোর্ট** with week stepper, filters, print/CSV, and per-student wa.me buttons carrying the **guardian-identical rendered message** (`.wa` variant) — the manual bridge to the ~129 contact-only families (ADR-003). **Verified green so far: vocab verifier (+2 checks), shared build, server tsc, app tsc, homeworkWeeklyDigest (new) + notificationsScheduler (+7) + graphqlDocuments suites; full server run + expo web export running as this entry is written — commit follows only on green.** Now/next: land #516 (Part A) → land this → test on the dev site (drive a submit pass, watch 17:30, and on the next Thursday check a guardian inbox + the report's wa.me copy), then promote both with the rest of the dev batch.

D-#355 preserved everywhere (already-CHASE is a strict no-op; auto mechanisms only ever do 0→1, so ≥2 attention / ≥3 parent-comms stay teacher-reachable only); ABSENT_REDELIVER never auto-chased; guardian pushes ride D-#260's per-day dedupe. New `trackers/homeworkDueDate.ts` (pure walk + `resolveHomeworkDueDate[ByItem]` seam; window-aware via `isLiveOn`, holidays prefetched, 21-day cap → next non-holiday school day fallback; no backfill of existing dueDates) wired into issue/redelivery/resubmission. **Verified green: server tsc; suites homeworkDueDate (new, 11), homeworkChaseSweep (new, 7), rosterPass/revert/scheduler extended; full server run 2952/2953 — the ONE failure (`expectedErrorRegistry`: unregistered `CommentImportError`) is the OWNER'S UNCOMMITTED monthly-comment-exchange work sitting in this shared checkout (untracked `MonthlyCommentExchangeService.ts` + reports/index/Audit edits, NOT staged, NOT in the PR), so PR CI is the clean gate.** Now/next: **Part B on `feat/hw-weekly-digest` after A lands** — HW_WEEKLY_DIGEST vocab kind + `homework.weeklyDigest.*` templates, `HomeworkWeeklyDigestService` (Sun–Wed unsubmitted per OWED_BY_STUDENT_STATES + Layer-A Thursday heads-up), the last-open-day-17:00 scheduler family (240-min stale window, `HWWD:{week}:{student}:{guardian}` dedupe), guardian deep-link into ChildHomework, and the Principal/Office weekly report screen with wa.me lines for the ~129 contact-only families. Plan file: `~/.claude/plans/declarative-questing-walrus.md`.

- 2026-08-04 (cont. 3): **SHIPPED TO DEV — the class-note cover overlay, the holiday admin screen, and the observation-draft invariants (PR #514).** Closes the list the owner confirmed with “all of them”. **(1) Class notes now follow whoever TAUGHT the period.** `publishClassNote` already let a cover write the note, but neither seam that CHASES it did: `PendingAlertService` (the red Today backlog) and `unwrittenClassNoteSlots` (shared by the CLASS_NOTE_PROMPT push ladder AND the in-app prompt). So the absent teacher was chased for a lesson they did not teach and the person who stood in front of the class was never asked — measured on prod, **40 of 40** covered periods in 60 days named the wrong teacher (`diag-classnote-cover-misdirect.ts`, read-only; 35 got a note anyway, the 5 without are Thu 6 Aug, a cover already scheduled, so this lands before that day). Both seams swap in BOTH directions, keyed per (slot, DAY) so covering one sitting never hands over the slot’s other days; `unwrittenClassNoteSlots` filters by teacher AFTER the overlay, which is what makes the covered-in direction reachable. **(2) Holidays are configurable from the app at last** — Routine → ছুটির দিন (`routine:manage`): upcoming/past split at today, add a day or a range, remove. `createHolidayException` had existed since R-1 wired to NO screen, which is why declaring 2026-08-05 needed a database script and why prod held zero holiday rows. Removing RETIRES (`active:false`, D-#449) — all seven read sites already filter `active:true`, so one flag withdraws the closure everywhere and the record of a day declared shut then reopened survives. **(3) The observation autosave** shipped unverified and still cannot be driven here (no test runner in the app workspace, no renderer/jsdom/browser automation), so the wiring it depends on is locked instead: user-scoped draft key, clear-on-successful-submit, snapshot never in the submit path, and useFormDraft’s write-after-restore gate — each proven to bite by sabotaging it and watching the test fail. **Verified green: 170 suites / 2925 server tests, server+shared+app tsc, vocab verifier, the GraphQL document gate (which is what proves `retireHolidayException` is registered and its documents match), `expo export --platform web` exit 0, and the CI gate on the PR.** Now/next: **NOTHING IS PROMOTED** — dev holds this plus the schema gate, exam editing, guardian DUE wording, both collapsibles, the permission-gate fix, the HW drill-by-card and the undeclared-classes card. Say “promote” for a `dev → main` PR. Two things still need a human on the dev site: type into an observation review, reload, confirm the text returns; and after Thu 6 Aug check the five covering teachers got their class-note alerts.

- 2026-08-04 (cont.): **BUILT — the answer-script archive, ALL slices AR-1..AR-4, branch `feat/script-archive` (PR → dev).** Same-day follow-through on the morning's PRD. App: 4 screens in the **ClassTest stack** (deliberate: the tab's `tracker:read || roster:manage` gate IS the archive's audience — no new drawer group, no AC-1 blind spot), archive hub link on the class-test home, and the batched `archiveLocationsForTests` lookup line ("স্ক্রিপ্ট কোথায়? BX-2026-05 · অফিস আলমারি তাক ২ / ফাইল করা হয়নি + ফাইল action") on home rows + dashboard drill-down — one query per screen over the visible ids, never per row. **Verified green: vocab verifier (+11 AR checks), server tsc, archive jest suite 23/23, app tsc, full server suite incl. the document gate.** Now/next: merge to dev, test on the dev site (create the real boxes per class, file one real bundle end-to-end), then promote.

Numbering note: D-#442 was already claimed by a CHANGELOG row with no DECISIONS entry, so this session's rows start at D-#443. **Now/next: build AR-1** (vocab + models + minted boxCode + file/lookup + the "স্ক্রিপ্ট কোথায়?" line on the class-test screens) in a separate session, branch from `dev`.

- 2026-08-03 (cont. 2): **SHIPPED TO PROD — the pipeline's FIRST step and the reviewer's missing content (PRs #493→#494, #497→#499).** The owner opened the image queue on prod, found an empty book picker, and asked the question that reordered the work: *shouldn't someone upload the book.json first, then it gets assigned for review, and shouldn't the reviewer see the book, the chapters, everything in the JSON, and comment on each item?* Right on all counts, and two of the three gaps were real defects rather than ordering preferences. **(1) NO WAY IN.** Every screen shipped so far is downstream of a book existing and the only route in was a terminal mutation. New **বই আপলোড** screen: pick a `.json` or paste it, decide STRUCTURALLY which shape it is (both a book.json and a §5 patch envelope carry `book_id` + `lessons`; only a patch carries `patch_id` — not trusting `schema_version`, because what decides how it is submitted is what the object actually contains), then hand it to the SAME `submitSupportBookPatch` the desktop path uses (D-#408). A full book.json is WRAPPED, and the wrap is SHOWN before submitting rather than done invisibly: the patch id is the permanent name of that submission, `(bookId, patchId)` is uniquely indexed, and a repeat upload is refused on purpose — so the author names it, not a generated timestamp. A RED renders as the OUTCOME, not an error (findings REDs-first with check, severity and location; the patch is stored either way), and `policyMissing` shows in warning colour because a patch validated against an incomplete policy set passed a weaker test than the author thinks (D-#403). **(2) THE REVIEWER COULD NOT READ THE LESSON (D-#440).** `blocks` have been stored verbatim since SB-1 and nothing exposed them — `supportBookLessons` returned `blockCount`, a NUMBER — so the review screen asked for a verdict against the §7 checklist over text nobody could see: the decorative-checklist failure this module exists to prevent, reproduced inside it. New `supportBookLessonContent` returns blocks and slots in reading order, one lesson at a time (a 54-lesson book is 764 KB of JSON), PART-TYPED and PART-JSON so a frozen renderer's schema is not mirrored into a second contract here. **(3) NO PER-ITEM COMMENT (D-#440).** The verdict had one feedback box for 493 blocks, and the only item-anchored channel was escalation, which means *take this to the senior* — making every wrong byline an escalation floods the inbox or, more likely, trains reviewers to stop raising things. `BookItemComment` is the ordinary reviewer→author channel, reusing `ESCALATION_TARGETS` rather than mirroring it. **Comments are RESOLVABLE and an unresolved one BLOCKS SIGN-OFF** — my call, stated as an assumption when the owner said "start and finish all the remaining": a note nobody has to answer is a note that gets skipped in a busy week, and resolved means the change is IN, not that the note was read. Resolving edits no lesson field; text still moves only through a validated patch. **The static resolver-gate guard earned its keep (D-#441):** the resolve mutation was first written as a `book:read` field gate with the OR as an `if` in the resolver body, and the guard failed it as an ungated mutation — correctly, because a check hidden in a body is one no guard can see. Fixed with a new `hasAnyPermission` scope (Pothos's `$any` combines DIFFERENT scopes, so it cannot express an OR over the same one), and the guard TIGHTENED in the same change: it reads the authScopes block only, and every permission in an OR must be a write. Gates: shared build, server+app typecheck, **jest 2859/2859 (166 suites, 16 new)**, expo web export (3867 modules, full output captured), **21/21 support-book GraphQL documents validated against the real schema**. Prod verified after deploy: `/healthz` 200, and both new fields answer "Not authorized" while a deliberate misspelling answers "Cannot query field". Adding the comment read to `signOffLesson` broke `supportBookReview.test.ts` until its `BookItemComment` mock was added — an unmocked model buffers against a connection that suite never opens, so it HANGS rather than fails; same regression shape as the PolicySetSnapshot memo. **NOT DONE — still the one thing I cannot supply: no screen has been driven by hand in a browser.** The book plane is configured on PROD only, so the click-through happens there. **Now / next:** upload the real C1-BAN `book.json` through বই আপলোড — that is now possible and is the only way to exercise the rest — then grant the actual illustrator/reviewer/senior/assembler their `book:*` permissions in Access Control. Activating the governance documents still has NO screen (`activateSupportBookPolicy` is terminal-only), so expect `policyMissing` to come back non-empty on the first upload; that is the next gap. SB-6 model bake-off and ST-1..ST-5 storybook slices remain unstarted.

- 2026-08-03 (cont.): **SHIPPED TO PROD — the four support-book production screens + the book-plane backup (PRs #485 → #486).** The module had a complete, commissioned server and NO screens: every step of the runbook was a terminal mutation, which is fine for commissioning and useless for the 201 image slots C1-BAN needs at 2–4 generations each. **Illustrator's queue** (prompt to copy, refs, aspect, living-being flag, the four lineage chips, four one-tap stage uploads) defaults to OUTSTANDING-ONLY — a list of 201 rows where 190 are done is a list nobody scans — refetches after an upload because an upload can make everything downstream stale and only the server walks the chain (D-#417), and never fetches `complianceNote` (stripe language must not reach a prompt someone can paste, README §5). **Reviewer:** lessons expand in place with round history, the §7 checklist as chips, a verdict, and the escalate form INSIDE the same card, because handing an item up must not mean abandoning the lesson you were reading (D-#410); self-reviewed rounds are badged, which is the entire point of stamping them (D-#424). **Senior inbox:** oldest-first, unchanged from the server's order (the thread waiting longest is blocking a lesson); resolve renders only for a `book:review_senior` holder and says in words that a ruling moves NO text in the book — a senior who believes otherwise closes threads and wonders why nothing happened. **Assembler:** the gate and every reason BEFORE the button, Principal-only force offered only after a refusal, render history with every job's PDFs still downloadable, and `book.json` export (D-#406). **THE GATE HAD TO BE FIXED FIRST (D-#438):** app navigation has always asked `roleHasPermission(role, …)` — a question about a ROLE — which is blind to per-user grants; `book:*` sits only on the PRINCIPAL template (D-#405) and every illustrator/reviewer/assembler is ordinary staff with an AC-1 grant, so template gating would have hidden every book screen from every person who works on books. New `myPermissions` root query returns the caller's own effective set on the SAME round-trip as `me`; `useAuth().can()` is the gate for new screens. It grants nothing — resolvers still call `callerHasPermission` themselves. Screens mount in the Admin stack rather than a fifth tab (D-#439): book production is seasonal project work for a handful of people, and the tab bar is the daily surface for the whole school. **Backup:** the nightly dump covered `MONGODB_URI` only, leaving the separate book mongod unbacked-up — now its own dump to its own Drive subfolder with its own rotation, non-fatal and skipped entirely when `BOOK_MONGODB_URI` is unset (D-#285: a nightly FAILED line for a thing that does not exist trains people to ignore the log). Gates: shared build, server+app typecheck, **jest 2833/2833 (165 suites)**, expo web export (3865 modules, full output captured — a tail hides bundler failures), **every book GraphQL document validated against the real 8183-line schema** (the app has no codegen, so a field the server does not expose typechecks fine and fails only at runtime), and the backup block EXECUTED on prod against real Drive (dumped, uploaded, rotated). Prod verified after deploy: `/healthz` + `/readyz` 200, `{ myPermissions }` answers "Not authorized" while a misspelling answers "Cannot query field", and the served bundle contains `supportBookAssemblyGate` / `BookEscalationInbox` / `sbQueueTitle`. **NOT DONE — the one thing I cannot supply: no screen has been driven by hand in a browser.** The book plane is configured on PROD only, so the click-through has to happen there, logged in as Principal. **Now / next:** click through the four screens on prod; then load a real book (C1-BAN) and grant the actual illustrator/reviewer/senior/assembler their `book:*` permissions in Access Control; the SB-6 model bake-off and the ST-1..ST-5 storybook slices remain unstarted.

- 2026-08-03: **Prod data fix + the UI gap behind it, plus two UI asks.** (1) **Dayeef Elahi HW-C3-SCI-0012** (Class 3 Science, given 2026-07-30) was marked submitted + checked by mistake; reverted to **DUE** in `scdhub_prod` by calling the real `revertHomeworkRecord` twice (dry-run first, `_id`-pinned, two HW_RECORD_REVERTED audit rows with the Principal as actor, 17 sibling records untouched). (2) **Why it needed a script:** the workspace offered the D-#338 undo on SUBMITTED rows and on the same-day RETURNED list but NOT on CHECKED/RESUBMIT — the audit log shows the teacher had already hit that wall (she undid her own RETURNED stamp on 08-02 and stopped at CHECKED). The ৩ ফেরত step now exposes the same undo in BOTH the homework and assignment workspaces. (3) **Header title** no longer runs under 🔔/👤 — react-navigation caps the title with a hard-coded 72px-per-side reservation and native-stack will not forward `headerTitleContainerStyle`, so HeaderTitle bounds the text (measured in the running app: 224px cap, 52px clearance). (4) **Observation review autosaves a local draft** (new `useFormDraft`) so a reload or dropped connection no longer loses an observer's typing. **Now / next:** branch `fix/tracker-revert-and-header` is up as a PR to dev — the autosave is the one piece NOT driven end to end (the seeded Principal is not an assigned observer locally), so exercise type → reload → restore on the dev site before promoting to main.

- 2026-08-02 (cont. 2): **Routine versioning built (D-#436), the recruitment load analysis, and two UI fixes.** (1) **Routine edits no longer overwrite the past.** The model has carried `[effectiveFrom, effectiveTo)` since D-#47(3) and every read resolves by date, but no WRITE ever closed a window — all 225 prod slots read `effectiveFrom 2026-01-01` open-ended, so "what was the routine in June?" was unanswerable. `updateRoutineSlot` now closes the old row at `endOfDayBefore(effectiveFrom)` and opens a replacement; `deleteRoutineSlot` retires rather than deletes; both take an optional `effectiveFrom` (default today) and write ROUTINE_SLOT_REVISED/RETIRED audit rows. New `modules/routine/liveWindow.ts` is the single place the window predicate is written, and the read sites that meant "current" now carry it — while LOOK-BACK readers (attendance backlog, pending alerts) deliberately do NOT, applying the window per date instead. `unbindIfOrphaned` is window-aware as of the changeover date. (2) **`docs/teacher-load-analysis-2026-08.md`** — 14 of 22 teacher profiles carry all 225 periods/week; the P1–P3 track wall leaves 75 idle general mornings against 75 idle religious afternoons (46% utilisation, zero morning float); July lost 17.3 school-days to leave and only 55% of the 62 stranded lessons got a confirmed cover, with **every Arabic failure at a P3 window where no free teacher can teach Arabic** — propped up 4 times by Akter Hossen, who is to stop taking classes. Hamida out / Jerin in nets to 14 staff. Class 6 = +1 general teacher; each boys/girls split = +1 more (+1 to +7). (3) Class-test results/publish cards now name the class; (4) the class-note report table is legible in dark mode (it painted a hard-coded light surface but took text colour from the theme). **Now / next:** the routine → assignment-teacher prompt is BLOCKED on what "assignment teacher" means (the teaching ScopeGrant, which the routine already syncs automatically, or a separate tracker owner) and needs effective-dating on `ScopeGrant` for its "if yes, change date".

- 2026-08-02 (cont. 2): **BUILT — SB-5 (rationale reads) and SB-6 SERVER SIDE (in-app authoring chat), branch `feat/support-book-sb1`.** SB-6 is scoped to the server deliberately: the owner will use **Claude Desktop for the support-book JSON for the next few months**, so an Expo chat UI would sit unused while ageing. Everything built is testable now and the UI is the only remaining piece. **The provider is a SEAM, not a choice** — which model writes Bengali well enough for a taught-letter inventory is empirical, and the validator's RED count is already a free objective scorer, so the model is config (`BOOK_AUTHOR_MODEL`) defaulting to the **existing `GEMINI_API_KEY`**; the seam follows `CommentProvider` (D-#399) including its hard-won details: an alias resolves to a dated model and THAT is what a bad batch is traced to, `thought` parts are reasoning and never the answer, and a non-STOP finish is a truncated reply that must be rejected rather than merged. **THE ORDER OF THE PROMPT IS THE FEATURE:** policy first and byte-stable, then book, then lesson, then the turn — prompt caching is a strict PREFIX match, so a prefix that varies still WORKS and simply costs full price every turn, surfacing on an invoice months later rather than in a test. Tests assert the prefix is byte-identical across turns, that the lesson is NOT in it, and that prefix hashes match. Policy goes in **verbatim** (D-#403), since a paraphrase drifts from what the Principal approved, and the preamble tells the model **it is not the gate** — a model that believes its output ships argues with the validator instead of fixing the text. **One write path holds** (D-#408): a patch turn hands the envelope to the same `submitPatch` a Desktop upload uses, `source: IN_APP_CHAT` is recorded and branched on nowhere, and a RED returns findings with the session left OPEN. Every turn records model + resolved model + `policySetHash` + prompt version + usage; a per-book monthly ceiling refuses in Bangla, checked BEFORE the turn so one turn may overshoot — deliberate, since cost is unknowable until the call returns and refusing on an estimate stops an author mid-chapter for no reason. Gates: shared build, **vocab verifier PASS**, server+shared typecheck clean, **jest 2779/2779 (163 suites, 31 new across SB-5+SB-6)**. **NOT built:** the Expo chat UI, and SB-7 (image API) which the owner deferred — images stay ChatGPT-desktop then upload, which SB-2 already delivers end to end. **Owed before SB-6 is worth switching on:** the bake-off — 3-5 real পাঠ through both model families on the same policy prompt, compared on first-emit RED count, since C1-BAN and the governance docs were both produced against Claude and Gemini is untested for this task. **Now / next:** the first live render (two VM env lines + `npm install` in `book-pipeline/`), then the bake-off.

- 2026-08-02 (cont.): **SHIPPED TO DEV — SB-1..SB-4 merged (PR #467, CI green). Then BUILT SB-5 (rationale reads).** The merge needed a **D-# collision resolved**: a concurrent session had taken #426–#429 on dev for observation-AI and tracker decisions while this branch used them for the anchor gate, the validator survey, the nukta divergence and the snap `/tmp` finding — dev is shared truth and mine had not merged, so **mine were renumbered to #431–#434** across rows, docs, code comments and both logs, only where the reference was mine. (#281/#287/#354/#355/#369 stay duplicated on dev from earlier sessions; pre-existing, left alone.) **Deploy is safe:** `connectBookDb()` is not called at boot, so without `BOOK_MONGODB_URI` every book resolver returns a clean Bangla "not configured" and nothing else changes. **SB-5 was re-scoped after building SB-1..SB-4**, because the PRD's framing ("build the read surface") had already been overtaken — `BookEvent` has been written since SB-1 and `supportBookTimeline` already read it. What was actually missing was three things: **(1) per-ITEM timelines** — the `targetType`/`targetId` index existed but nothing queried it, so "why does THIS BLOCK read this way" was unanswerable though the data was there; **(2) policy text at a hash** — every event carried a `policySetHash` that proved two things shared a policy but could not say WHAT it was, which is the whole of D-#403 sitting behind one missing lookup, now closed by a `PolicySetSnapshot` memo written upsert-on-sight so a hash can never exist in the wild without a row explaining it, plus `resolvePolicySet` returning the versions AND their bodies with `supersededSince` flagged (the case where quoting today's text would mislead); **(3) actor names** — a log of "actor 6512ab…" is not a record anyone can read, and reading it years later is the point. `BookActorService` is the ONE place a book read crosses to identity: batched, by id only, at the resolver layer as D-#404 permits, and it **never throws** — a name lookup failing must not take down the timeline it decorates. Also pinned: an unknown id reads "(unknown account)" rather than blank, because a deleted staff account is a real answer and an empty string renders as a gap that looks like a bug. Regression caught: adding the memo broke five merge tests, which mock every book model and now needed `PolicySetSnapshot` — unmocked it buffers against an unopened connection and times out rather than failing fast. Gates: shared build, **vocab verifier PASS**, server+shared typecheck clean, **jest 2762/2762 (162 suites, 14 new)**. **Now / next:** SB-6 (in-app LLM authoring chat) and SB-7 (in-app image API) — both deliberately last (D-#412); or the first live render, which still needs the two VM env lines and `npm install` in `book-pipeline/`.

- 2026-08-02: **BUILT — SB-4 worker loop + SSE; SB-4 is now COMPLETE except a live render, branch `feat/support-book-sb1` (worktree).** `BookBuildWorker` (claim → materialize → gather compliant images from Drive → render → file the PDFs → mark the job) plus `server/src/workers/bookBuildWorker.ts` as its own process (`npm run worker:book`), and `GET /book-builds/:jobId/stream` for the live log. **The SSE design point worth remembering:** the worker is a SEPARATE process (D-#407), so it cannot publish into the API's memory — the log is appended to `BookBuildJob.log` with an **aggregation-pipeline `$concat`** (atomic; a read-modify-write would drop chunks because `onLog` fires un-awaited) and the route TAILS that field. Two things fall out free: a reconnecting browser gets the whole log from byte 0, and the log survives the worker dying. `X-Accel-Buffering: no` is set because Caddy and Cloudflare both buffer by default, which would defeat streaming entirely. **THE BUG THE TESTS FOUND:** a job whose render reported success but whose PDFs could not be read was marked **SUCCEEDED with an empty outputs list** — the assembler would be told the book was built, with nothing to download. It now FAILS with a reason. It surfaced only because the first version of the test had a runner that PRINTED paths instead of writing files, leaving the entire upload half untested; the runner now writes real files, so `readPdf` and the upload path actually execute. Also pinned: images are keyed by the FILENAME `book.json` names (not slot id — the renderer resolves the filename); a slot with no `filename` is skipped because the validator owns that judgement and duplicating it would create two sources of truth; Drive being down records on the job and **keeps the queue draining** rather than losing the worker; and the work directory is cleaned in a `finally` (half a gigabyte of PNGs per abandoned render fills the disk in a week). The worker requeues stuck jobs on boot, since a killed worker leaves a RUNNING row that blocks the queue forever, and SIGTERM **finishes the current job** rather than killing a render mid-PDF. *Process note:* a `python - <<PY` block with relative paths ran after the shell reset to the main repo and edited **`server/package.json` + `server/src/index.ts` in the MAIN checkout**; caught immediately, diffed to confirm the changes were only mine, reverted with `git checkout --`, and re-applied to the worktree with absolute paths. Gates: shared build, **vocab verifier PASS**, server+shared typecheck clean, **jest 2731/2731 (161 suites, 14 new)**. **STILL NOT DONE — the one thing tests cannot supply:** no book has been rendered end to end. That needs the two `.env` lines on the VM (`PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH`, `BOOK_WORK_ROOT`), `npm install` inside `book-pipeline/`, and a real book with compliant images. **Now / next:** land SB-1..SB-4, then a live render on the dev site.

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 8): **BUILT — SB-4 assembly orchestration, branch `feat/support-book-sb1` (worktree). PARTIAL BY DESIGN: the renderer itself is unverified until the VM has Chromium.** `BookBuildJob` + `BookBuildService` + `BookRenderRunner` + four resolvers. **The gate runs before anything expensive** — a render is minutes of Chromium, so refusing a doomed build at queue time costs nothing and names the actual reason while the person is still looking at it: stale artifacts **by file** (D-#417), unresolved escalations (ANSWERED included), an empty scope — and it reports **every** reason at once, because fixing one blocker only to be told about the next is the worst version of a gate. `force` is **PRINCIPAL-only** and recorded on the job and in the timeline, never silent. **Concurrency is 1**, enforced by an atomic claim rather than by convention, because the constraint is the host's memory and the VM has no swap (D-#423); `requeueStuckJobs` makes a killed worker recoverable instead of wedging the queue. `materializeBookJson` writes the SCHEMA's snake_case field names — camelCase would silently produce a book the frozen CLI cannot read — and the same function is the **export escape hatch** (D-#406), so the app can never become the only way to build a book. The runner spawns the vendored CLI with an **argument array and `shell:false`** (the paths carry a book id and Bangla filenames), runs **validate first** so a book failing a JSON check never reaches Chromium, and fails the WHOLE job if either edition fails (ASSEMBLY §5). **The spawn seam is injected, so the sequencing tests are real tests of real logic** rather than mocks of the thing under test — 24 tests cover the gate, the queue, materialization and ordering without needing a browser. `RenderError` was deleted before it existed as dead code: a failed render is a RESULT (`ok:false` + a reason), not an exception, and an unused error class would still need a D-#387 alerting decision. **NOT DONE and needed before SB-4 can be called finished:** (1) the pipeline is **not yet vendored** into `/book-pipeline/` — `BOOK_PIPELINE_ROOT` points at it and the copy (248 KB of JS + 1.5 MB of OFL fonts) is a deliberate commit I have not made unilaterally; (2) **no end-to-end render has run** — that needs `chromium-browser` + `PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH` + `python3-pil` on the VM (D-#413/#423), verified **under the systemd unit** not an interactive shell; (3) the worker LOOP and the SSE endpoint are not written — the claim/render/upload pieces exist, the process that drives them does not. Gates: shared build, **vocab verifier PASS**, server+shared typecheck clean, **jest 2705/2705 (159 suites, 24 new)**. **Now / next:** vendor the pipeline + prepare the VM, then the worker loop; or land SB-1..SB-4 first.

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 7): **BUILT — SB-3 review + escalation chain, branch `feat/support-book-sb1` (worktree).** `BookReviewRound` (one open round per পাঠ, the D-#40 guard) + `BookEscalation` (anchored to an ITEM — a block or a slot, not a book, because the disputes are specific). **The checklist IS the sign-off:** README §7's seven items are vocab, and `checklistPassed` goes true only on APPROVE with EVERY item ticked — a partially-ticked list that still signs off makes the checklist decorative, which is the failure mode of every checklist not mechanically enforced. **Reviewer ≠ author**, except a PRINCIPAL, whose round is STAMPED `selfReviewed` and carried onto the lesson (D-#424) — the rule's purpose is that a later reader can tell whether a second pair of eyes saw the lesson, which a stamp answers and a refusal does not. **A resolution changes NO lesson field** (D-#410): the senior writes the ruling, the AUTHOR then submits a patch citing it through the same validator, so there stays exactly one write path and the ruling and its application are two separately visible events — pinned by a test that snapshots the lesson around a resolve. Sign-off refuses while any escalation is unresolved, and an **ANSWERED escalation counts as unresolved** because someone still has to apply the ruling. A senior's reply ANSWERS, a further reply re-OPENS, indefinitely. Two design details worth keeping: the round snapshots `artifactPatchId` at assignment, so a re-merge mid-review is visible rather than silently changing the thing under review; and `isSenior` on a reply is **derived from the caller's grants**, never client-supplied, or a reviewer could mark their own escalation answered. Seven resolvers (assign/submit/raise/reply/resolve/sign-off + two inboxes, senior's oldest-first), four audit kinds, `ReviewRuleError` registered as an expected error. Gates: shared build, **vocab verifier PASS**, server+shared typecheck clean, **jest 2677/2677 (158 suites, 19 new)**. **NOT built:** SB-4..SB-7. **Now / next:** SB-4 (render worker) needs the VM Chromium + `python3-pil` (D-#413/#423) — the first slice with a deploy-time dependency; or land SB-1..SB-3.

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 6): **BUILT — SB-2 image pipeline complete, branch `feat/support-book-sb1` (worktree).** `BookImageAsset` + `BookImageService` hold the slot chain **APPROVED → CROPPED → UPSCALED → COMPLIANT**, each stage recording a fingerprint of what it was DERIVED FROM, so re-approving an image makes downstream rows disagree with reality and **the disagreement IS the staleness** — nothing has to remember to invalidate anything. A re-upload **supersedes rather than overwrites** (a rejected image is evidence). `StoredFile` gains four `book_*` kinds; `DriveStore` takes a **nested** subfolder path plus Drive `appProperties`, so `books/<BOOK_ID>/<stage>/` files identify themselves from the Drive side. `POST /files/book` uploads and **registers the artifact on the same request** — registering is what makes downstream stale, so it can never be a follow-up a client forgets. Three resolvers: the illustrator's slot queue (prompt, refs, per-stage lineage, `hasStale`), the book-wide stale report, and a slot's full history including superseded rows. **THE BUG THE TESTS CAUGHT is the whole point of the slice: staleness did not CASCADE.** Comparing each stage to its direct input marks only CROPPED stale — UPSCALED still matches the cropped FILE, which did not change, only its standing did — so a per-stage check reported UPSCALED and COMPLIANT **FRESH** and the stale picture would have reached print, which is exactly what D-#417 exists to stop. `chainStates()` now walks the chain once and carries staleness downward. Also pinned: an artifact whose input **vanished** is stale (a naive compare says fresh — there is nothing to compare to), a MISSING stage does **not** make its downstream stale, and the stale report names **files, not stages**. **Doctrine enforced by omission:** `compliance_note` is never read by any resolver — telling an illustrator where the stripe lands is the surest way to get one drawn — and the guard asserts the ACCESS (`.compliance_note`), not the word, since the comments explaining its absence would trip a substring match. **A second-order fix worth noting:** the `GET /files/:id` dispatch first used the exported book-kind list and broke a dozen suites that mock the StoredFile module; it now dispatches on the `book_` PREFIX, with a test pinning that invariant in both directions rather than assuming it. Gates: server+shared typecheck clean, **vocab verifier PASS**, **jest 2650/2650 (157 suites, 28 new in SB-2)**. **NOT built:** SB-3..SB-7. **Now / next:** SB-3 (review + escalation chain), or land SB-1+SB-2 — `BOOK_MONGODB_URI` and the VM `mongod` are still deploy-time.

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 5): **BUILT — SB-1 is COMPLETE: validator port, merge service and resolvers, branch `feat/support-book-sb1` (worktree).** Continues cont. 4's foundation. **Validator port (D-#432/#428)** — ported from TWO sources because they disagree about what exists: six checks from `validate-studybook.js`, the letter audit from `SB-Governance/validator_letter_audit.py`, plus the five checks README §6 declares that upstream never implemented. Every check records its **provenance (CLI | PY | README)** so a divergence from the pipeline is visible, and the image DPI floor is **explicitly skipped with a reason** (an assembly gate — bytes live in Drive at merge time) because an unrun check must never read as a pass. **Running it against the real 54-lesson C1-BAN book found two defects in my own port that review would not have:** a bare `strip` match flagged a *striped tiger*, a *strip of tablets* and a *strip of grass* (now qualified matching, all three pinned), and **29 of 37 letter-audit REDs were a decomposed nukta** — ড় ঢ় য় are single letters in the inventory but circulate as base + nukta, and Unicode's composition-exclusion list means NFC never recomposes them, so "fix the text, never widen the allowlist" cannot apply and the reference can only ever pass on one spelling (**D-#433**, the one deliberate divergence; a nukta is admitted only where its composed letter is taught). Real-book RED **42 → 9**, and the 9 are genuine: one lesson missing codes, three stray `/`, five conjuncts at পাঠ the inventory's own `open_items` flags as unresolved. Tests carry the **Python reference's own 8-case seeded-error test** — the README §6 gate — through the port, with an inline fixture rather than importing the 43 KB governance file. **Merge service** — `submitPatch` builds the merge CANDIDATE (validating the patch alone would miss inventory order, duplicate lesson_no and layout refs), refuses on RED but **stores the patch anyway** because a refused report is the informative part, merges wholesale by `lesson_no`, and stamps `policySetHash`. Two bugs its tests caught: the supersede chain read `currentPatchId` off the document **after** the update overwrote it (a patch linked to itself), and merging must **not** move a lesson's workflow state or a reviewer's sign-off is silently undone. **Resolvers** — the gate boundary; the services carry no permission checks by design. Four audit kinds added to the union (security log) alongside `BookEvent` (editorial log, D-#411). A **static guard** enumerates every `builder.*Field` and asserts each declares a `book:*` authScopes, checks the plane is configured, and reaches no identity model — static because an ungated field is a mistake of OMISSION, and a runtime test only probes the fields someone remembered. `PatchRejectedError` deleted as dead code (a RED is returned, never thrown); `PatchShapeError` registered as expected. **Gates: shared build, vocab verifier PASS, server+shared tsc clean, jest 2616/2616 (155 suites, 50 new across SB-1).** **NOT built:** SB-2..SB-7. **Now / next:** SB-2 (image pipeline + lineage), or land SB-1 first — `BOOK_MONGODB_URI` + the VM `mongod` are still deploy-time and nothing so far needs them.

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 4): **BUILT — SB-1 foundation: the book plane exists, branch `feat/support-book-sb1` (worktree).** First code for the support-book module after D-#403–#427. Shipped: `/shared/vocab.ts` **section A.18** (BOOK_TYPES + the SCHEMA-verbatim mirrors + the workflow enums — app-native, no wire twin, subject on `ROUTINE_SUBJECTS` and class on `ROSTER_CLASS_LEVELS` so ISLAM/Nursery need no locked-enum change); **seven `book:*` permissions**, none RESERVED, **all seven on the PRINCIPAL template** (D-#424) and **none on any other template** — AC-1 hands them out per user; **`server/src/bookDb.ts`**, a second mongoose connection opened lazily so the plane boundary is wiring rather than a remembered rule; and **five models on it** — `PolicyDoc` (governance as versioned DATA), `SupportBook` (bookType-agnostic per D-#420), `SupportBookLesson` (**one document per পাঠ** — what makes wholesale-by-lesson merge and parallel chapters safe), `LessonPatch` (append-only, both authoring paths, rejected patches retained because a refused validator report is often the most informative row in the timeline), `BookEvent` (the **editorial** log; `Audit` still answers who-did-what, D-#411). **`bookPlane.test.ts` proves the isolation structurally rather than by convention**: every book model registers off the default connection, the book connection knows no identity model, no book schema refs one, and the three unique merge keys exist. Two things the repo's own guards caught mid-build and both were right: mongoose rejected `[Schema.Types.Mixed]` for a typed mixed array (now `[{}]`), and the **D-#387 expected-error registry failed the suite** on an unclassified `BookDbNotConfiguredError` — registered as a deliberate fault, since only a deliberate `connectBookDb()` can raise it, so it is a misconfiguration to fix rather than user noise. **NOT built yet:** the validator port (D-#432 — port from `SB-Governance/validator_letter_audit.py` + the real inventory JSON, **not** from the stale SCHEMA §6), the merge service, and the resolvers. Gates run in this session: shared build, **vocab verifier PASS**, server + shared `tsc --noEmit` clean, **jest 2566/2566 (152 suites, 11 new)**. **Now / next:** the validator port + merge service, then resolvers; `BOOK_MONGODB_URI` and the VM `mongod` are deploy-time, not needed to continue.
- 2026-08-02 (cont. 1): **Class-test dashboard ordered + paged (D-#430).** Owner ask: complete-and-unpublished first, then incomplete-and-overdue, then incomplete, then complete-and-published, 50 a page. Built as four buckets over the existing mutually-exclusive `state` partition (so every row lands in exactly one), with newest-exam-first inside a bucket and most-days-late-first inside the overdue one; the pager is client-side over the already-fetched rows, hidden when everything fits, and its index clamps instead of resetting. Rule extracted to app/src/lib/ctDashboardOrder.ts and covered by 18 executed assertions. **Caveat:** scdhub_local holds ZERO class-test exams, so only the logic is verified — the rendering needs a look on the dev site after this deploys. **Now / next:** eyeball the CT dashboard on dev; nothing else in flight.

Owner asked whether the analysis + rota could be produced in-app by passing an instruction to Gemini. Answer specced as **the model chooses and narrates, never computes** — the server expands the routine into dated candidates (school days derived from active slots, levels 1–5, clock times from `dayStartMinutes` + grid durations) and the model returns candidate ids against a constraint echo the validator checks and the Principal reads. CO-14 reads the routine as truth, so these become visible warnings rather than silent wrong periods. **Now / next:** build CO-13/CO-14 per the slices, starting with the pure candidate expander + facts builder (both unit-testable with no API); clean the 2 test rows separately.
