- 2026-08-01 (cont. 2): **CORRECTED — the backup job was duplication, and the Drive card understated its ceiling ~40× (D-#425), branch `feat/system-health-trends`.** Both caught by the owner. **(a)** D-#416 built a weekly dump-to-Drive on the premise that no restore point existed. `scripts/backup.sh` has run **nightly from cron since 2026-06-30** (ADR-011) — `mongodump --archive --gzip` into `SCD-Hub-Backups` — with a restore runbook **tested by doing** (DEP-4 drill into `scdhub_dev`). All of it was in this repo the whole time; I looked in Drive only after being shown the folder. The dump job is **deleted** (not merely disabled) and SH-7 is now a read-only **monitor** of that folder. Critically the band reads the **newest archive's age alone, never the spacing between files**: `drive-backup.mjs` rotates 7 daily / 4 weekly / 3 monthly, so the folder legitimately looks weekly beyond the last week — I misread that thinning as "missed nights" before checking the rotation, which is exactly the false alarm a spacing check would raise every week. **(b)** The Drive card read `storageQuota.usage` (1.33 TB, spanning all Google services) against `storageQuota.limit` (100 TiB, the pooled-Workspace sentinel) and rendered **1.3% "Healthy"**, while the account's own Drive page shows **51.57 GB of 100 GB**. It now reads `usageInDrive` against a configured `DRIVE_LIMIT_GB` (default 100 GB) — **51.6%, the second-tightest ceiling**, not the non-issue D-#414 recorded — with trash (**11.61 GB, reclaimable**) on its own line. Verified live through the real services: backup band `ok`, newest archive today, 11 archives / 89.9 MB pool; Drive 51.57 GB of 100 GB. Gates: server + app typecheck clean, jest **2547/2547** (151 suites), expo web export exit 0. **The lesson worth keeping:** both errors came from trusting a remote API's self-description over cheaper, closer sources — the repo's own `scripts/` directory, and the screen the owner looks at every day. **Now / next:** PR #452 already merged SH-4..SH-7 into dev, so this correction needs its own PR before any promotion. **Free win still open:** emptying Drive trash returns ~11.6 GB.

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 3): **PLANNED — the storybook is IN: build contract `docs/prd-storybook.md`, slices ST-1..ST-5 (D-#421–#423). Docs-only; nothing built.** Owner ruling closing the clause D-#420 left open: "update prd to host the storybook in the system like study book." **It is a second `bookType`, not a second module** — no new plane, no new database, and **not one new permission**, because the seven `book:*` grants already cover author/illustrator/reviewer/senior/assembler for both lines. The two products differ in *content rules*, not *process shape* (both: author a JSON → generate images against reference sheets → apply a compliance strip → review → render → log why), so the per-type adapter supplies exactly four things — schema, validator check set, render profiles (**six** outputs for a storybook vs the সহায়িকা's two), policy doc set — and **nothing else in the engine may branch on type**. One genuinely new gate rides along (ST-3): the SOP's anchor verification is a **separation** rule, so `ANCHOR_STATES` carries four values with `LLM_CHECKED` deliberately **not** a pass ("an LLM 'verified' is a lead, not a verdict", SOP 2.3), the verifier **may not be the book's author** (the observer≠observed guard shape), and assembly locks on anything but `HUMAN_VERIFIED`. **D-#422 drops Upscayl for sharp** — measured, not assumed: it is a Windows `.exe` Vulkan/NCNN upscaler with no ARM64 Linux build, on a Neoverse-N1 host with only a virtio render node, making it the single storybook step that could not move to the server at all; sharp is what `studybook-pipeline` already uses, so both pipelines now share **one** server-runnable upscale, and the pipeline's existing minimum-dimension gate is the arbiter rather than preference (a visible quality drop on real artwork would be grounds to revisit — to be checked at ST-4, not assumed away). **D-#423, host cost, read live:** Python 3.12.3 is present but **Pillow is NOT** (`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'PIL'`), so `python3-pil` is the one genuine install; **one Chromium serves both renderers**, `pdffonts`/`soffice` are already there, and builds stay serialized at 1 — so the second book type adds **no memory peak**, only ~1 GB of disk against 86 GB free. That is why hosting both was cheap: the costly resources are per-**build**, not per-**pipeline**, and only one build ever runs. **The commercial surface stays excluded permanently** until its own PRD — no storefront, reader, DRM, pricing or payments, and nothing in the storybook contract prepares one. **Still open (prd-storybook §8):** the workbench **cutover** (`GB-B01` is mid-flight, `S4-B01` importing — the workbench keeps running and is replaced function by function, never in one cut), where finished storybooks surface for the school, who verifies anchors given verifier≠author on a small team, and whether `chapter-book-48` lands on the laptop first. Numbering: live max was **D-#420** (my own) when written. Nothing touched code, so no verifier applies. **Now / next:** unchanged — SB-1 is still the first build, and ST-1 cannot start before SB-1..SB-2 exist. **Late addition, D-#424** (owner: "principal can do everything, author, review etc"): all seven `book:*` permissions move onto the PRINCIPAL template, and — the part that actually needed deciding — **the same-person separations become a stamp rather than a wall for that one role**. Permissions were never the constraint here; the separations are **row-level** (reviewer ≠ author of *that* lesson, verifier ≠ author of *that* book) and holding every grant does not dissolve them. Three options existed: keep refusing (blocks a one-person operation outright), silently allow (a sign-off that reads as a second pair of eyes when it was the same pair — the worst, because the record becomes a lie), or allow-and-record. Allow-and-record wins: `selfReviewed`/`selfVerified` stamped automatically, shown with the sign-off, carried in the timeline — **the rule's purpose was always so a later reader can tell whether a second pair of eyes saw the work**, which a stamp answers honestly and a refusal does not answer at all. Unchanged: `HUMAN_VERIFIED` still needs a named human and timestamp (self-verification changes *who may*, never *whether a human did*), assembly still locks on an unverified anchor, and the separation stays enforced for every non-Principal role. No typed reason required — for a solo Principal it would read "nobody else available" on every book. This also **settles prd-storybook §8 Q3** (who verifies anchors on a small team).

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 2): **PLANNED — Support-Book PRD amended after a full read of the storybook project (D-#417–#420). Docs-only; nothing built.** Owner pointed at `C:\Users\HP\Downloads\Story Book` and asked how it too could be integrated. **The headline finding is that a working internal production app already exists there** — `workbench/` (Express + vanilla-JS SPA on `127.0.0.1:4321`, built over three phases in July): an eight-stage machine with entry/completion gates, in-app Gemini image generation with per-slot conversation threads and automatic reference attachment, multi-key rotation with 429/daily-quota handling, SSE log streaming, and lineage invalidation — **with five S1 books fully assembled through it**, `GB-B01` mid-flight and a `chapter-book-48` format specified the same day. It is a working prototype of most of what prd-support-book proposes, so the amendment adopts its proven ideas rather than inventing parallel ones. **D-#417 lineage + staleness is the gap that mattered**: the PRD had nothing saying "you re-approved this image, so its cropped/upscaled/compliant versions are stale and the build is locked" — and that failure is *silent*, because a stale image still produces a perfectly valid PDF, just with the old picture; one person catches it, five people across 201 slots do not. **D-#418** takes the workbench's rule that a human eyeball gate is real (`{by, at}` stored, never satisfied by a green exit code — its "the app never sets `anchor.verified`" principle, which here protects `reviewer_signoff.checklist_passed`) plus SSE logs so a fit-guard failure on lesson 31 surfaces *while* it fails. **D-#419 amends D-#409 on the owner's ruling** ("keep both — currently gpt desktop, later maybe api"): images get two permanent paths, external upload (SB-2, forever) and in-app API (SB-7), structured exactly like D-#408's two authoring paths so there is one code path downstream and `IMAGE_SOURCES` is recorded but branched on nowhere. D-#409's premise — that generation belongs outside the app — was disproven by the workbench doing it over five books. **D-#420, the governance one:** the owner re-scoped the storybook line as **school-first** ("designed for commercial use but now we will use it for school primarily"), which is what makes it eligible to share this module at all — the blocker was never technical, it was README §7's absolute that support books never enter a sales catalog, and with both lines school-first that rule's *purpose* is met. So SB-1..SB-7 is now specified **book-type-agnostic** (a design constraint on SB-1, not extra work in it), with schema/validator/profiles/policy-set behind a per-type adapter. New PRD §10 records what exists in the storybook project, what differs (slot not পাঠ; series canon not NCTB fidelity; fresh-eyes anchor verification; six PDFs not two; "QC = the chat itself", which does not survive a team), and what is deliberately **not** decided — whether `workbench/` is migrated, rewritten or left alone, and its own slice set. **No storefront, reader, pricing or payment surface enters this repo without its own PRD.** The workbench is producing books today and nothing here may stop it. Numbering: live max was **D-#416** when this was written (concurrent sessions hold #414–#416), so #417–#420 were taken — AGENTS rule 3, checked again. Nothing touched code, so no verifier applies. **Now / next:** the remaining prd §8 questions are still owner-owned (which book is next, senior reviewer identity, LLM spend ceiling, Drive retention, whether the strip chain stays local), then SB-1.

- 2026-08-01 (cont.): **BUILT — health trends, projection, heartbeat, prune list and backups (D-#416, SH-4..SH-7), branch `feat/system-health-trends`.** Owner ask after D-#414 shipped: charts, and earlier data. The useful move was not waiting a month for history — **every large collection carries a creation time** (`_id` embeds one regardless), so counts are walked backwards **exactly** and the chart opens with ~7 weeks of curve. Bytes are unrecoverable, so a backfilled day is priced at `count × today's bytes-per-doc`, flagged `estimated`, drawn muted **and** captioned. **The bug only a live run could find:** the first pass produced a 9 MB → 76 MB jump on deploy day, because a reconstructed day sums the tracked collections of ONE database while a measured day reports the whole-CLUSTER total the cap counts — two quantities on one axis, plus a projection fitted across the join. Fixed with a constant baseline for the unseen part; re-verified end to end against `scdhub_dev` (53 reconstructed days, continuous 70.4 → 76.5 MB, projection 2036-09-21). The projection **refuses to guess**: <3 points is not a trend, flat/shrinking has no date, past-limit is zero not negative, nulls are gaps. **SH-5:** ticker heartbeat on the panel (amber 150s / red 600s) with the consequence in words — a stall silently stops homework auto-DUE/auto-ISSUE, reminders, class-note prompts, sweeps, escalations. The heartbeat moved to a **leaf module**: the health service reports it while the scheduler imports the health service, and that cycle is one tsc compiles happily and Node resolves to `undefined` by load order. **SH-6:** prunable estimates from an ALLOWLIST — `audits` excluded by rule (ADR-008), school records excluded as product not exhaust, both pinned by tests; **report only, no prune button**. **SH-7:** weekly streamed gzip-NDJSON dump to Drive (no `mongodump` to provision), retention 4 touching only this job's files and running only after a successful upload, `ageDays` from the last SUCCESS, failures recorded — shipped **OFF behind `BACKUP_ENABLED=1`**. New `TrendSparkline` (View-based, no charting dep, matching MiniCharts). Gates: server+app typecheck clean, jest **2537/2537** (151 suites, 19 new), expo web export exit 0, 23 new BN/EN strings. Numbering: D-#415 was taken mid-build by a concurrent session, renumbered to D-#416 (AGENTS rule 3). **Still owed:** the screen has not been opened in the running app; prod history and the first real projection appear after the first ticker pass post-deploy. **To switch backups on:** add `BACKUP_ENABLED=1` to the prod service env — until then the panel correctly reports that no restore point exists. **Now / next:** PR into dev.

- 2026-08-01: **BUILT — the Principal can see free-tier headroom (D-#414, SH-1..SH-3), branch `feat/system-health`.** Owner ask: see the VM + MongoDB status in time to act. All three ceilings were MEASURED live before designing, and that changed the build twice. **Drive is not the risk** — it holds every uploaded byte (`StoredFile` is only a handle, D-#70) yet sits at 1.33 TB of a 100 TB pooled Workspace quota. **Atlas M0 is the ceiling that binds**, 76.2 MB of 512 MB, and the cap is per CLUSTER not per database: `scdhub_prod` 41.7 MB + `scdhub_local` 18.2 MB + `scdhub_dev` 16.4 MB, so **the two test copies eat ~45% of usage — production's own headroom**. A per-database panel would have hidden exactly that, so the read is cluster-wide, lists every database, and shows the current one's top 10 collections (contentartifacts 13.3 MB, importbatches 10.5 MB, notifications 17,786 rows). Shipped: Admin screen gated `audit:read` (Principal-only, **no new permission** — D-#281 precedent avoids the two-place sync); three cards with used/limit bars; bands 70% amber / 85% red; per-section errors so one dead probe never hides the rest; Drive cached 5 min. **Egress needed a design:** Linux counts only since boot, so a daily `NetSnapshot` (existing notification ticker, no new scheduler, above the school-day gate, idempotent on date key) turns counters into deltas — a reading LOWER than yesterday is a reboot, contributing its raw counter and flagging the month partial; one snapshot yields null, not a fake zero. Read-only by design: **no prune button** (deleting school data from a dashboard is a different decision). Gates: server+app typecheck clean, vocab verifier PASS, jest **2511/2511** (148 suites, 16 new), expo web export exit 0, and the REAL service run against the live cluster (output above). Numbering note: D-#403–#413 were already taken by the committed support-book contract, so this renumbered to D-#414 (AGENTS rule 3). Diag kept (read-only, untracked): `diag-db-usage.ts`, `diag-quota-probe.ts`, `diag-system-health.ts`. **Still owed:** the screen has not been opened in the running app; the VM numbers only become real once deployed (locally they report this laptop). **Actionable finding independent of the UI:** dropping/pruning the two test databases would free ~35 MB of the 512 MB cap. **Now / next:** PR into dev.

- 2026-08-01 (cont. 2): **BUILT — Monthly Report MR-6b (release notification) + MR-7 (the printable sheet). The slice line MR-1..MR-7 is now complete except the class roll-up.** **MR-6b:** a released report reaches the family on the existing rails — inbox notification for login-enabled guardians + wa.me click-to-send for every family with a phone — with **two wordings, not one**: a first release says "available", a re-release says "সংশোধিত". A family handed different numbers under an identical message has no way to know the report changed, which is the trust this feature cannot spend. Dedupe key is per REVISION so revision 2 notifies although revision 1 already did; bodies rendered ONCE per report from MT-1 and handed to the emitter pre-rendered (the N+1 guard); a phone-less family returns `unreachableByWa` rather than vanishing; **delivery never fails a release** (the release is already decided and audited — a transport failure is a follow-up). `MONTHLY_REPORT` notification kind synced three places. **MR-7:** `GET /pdf/monthly-report/:reportId` through the same pdfkit + NotoSansBengali engine (no headless browser — the Slice-1 Oracle constraint holds). **Nothing is recomputed** — every figure is read out of the frozen snapshot, so page, screen and guardian copy cannot drift; an **unreleased revision says so in bold**, so a draft printed for a desk check can never be handed over as the family's copy; the §4 gate is **re-asserted at the route** (a route is a second front door) so a narrowed teacher's sheet names its own limits and carries no other subject's row, no fee block and no paragraph, while a guardian may print only a RELEASED revision of their own child; the appendix prints the thresholds THIS revision used. Gates GREEN (executed): shared build; server typecheck; vocab verifier PASS; **full server jest 147 suites / 2471 tests** (+22 this stretch), including a real PDF render asserting magic bytes + embedded Bengali font. **STILL NOT DONE:** the Principal's **class-level roll-up** (the last piece of MR-7) — the per-student computation it needs already exists in `sectionMonthMetrics`. **Still never driven in a running app, and no report has been generated against real data;** GEMINI_API_KEY is set on local/dev/prod but **nothing has called Gemini yet**. **Now / next:** the class roll-up, then a live drive on the dev site (build a month for one section, review a comment, release, re-release, print), then the PR into `dev`.

- 2026-08-01 (cont.): **BUILT — Monthly Report MR-5b (staff UI) + MR-6a (the nightly sweep trigger), same branch `feat/monthly-report`.** **MR-5b:** `MonthlyReportConsoleScreen` (section × month, one row per child, bulk release) and `MonthlyReportDetailScreen` (the frozen numbers, trend chips, flags, the comment card). The screen **never decides releasability** — `releasable`/`blockedReason`/`requiresPrincipal` ride every row from the MR-3 pure verdict, so a disabled button and the refusal behind it cannot drift; only a releasable row is selectable; **bulk release names every refusal** instead of reporting one number (a child silently dropped from a batch is how a family never gets a report and nobody notices); the override-reason field appears only when a selected row actually needs it. Nothing is recomputed client-side — every figure is read out of `snapshotJson`, so screen, guardian copy and the future PDF cannot disagree. Both screens registered AFTER the param-free ones in the Reports stack (the initial-route param trap). **MR-6a:** `POST /triggers/monthly-report-sweep` behind the same shared secret + fail-closed rule as AT-4; a trigger rather than a write hook (a mark entered for 30 students would fire 30 recomputes of one class), idempotent because a revision is raised only when a printed figure moved, HARD_LOCKED months skipped, and one section's bad data cannot abort the sweep. Gates GREEN (executed): server typecheck; app typecheck exit 0; **expo web export exit 0 with the full log read**; monthly-report suites **104/104**; **10 new Bangla strings verified `\u`-escaped in the exported bundle** — note the bundle contains NO literal Bengali at all (55k `\u09xx` escapes), so probing for the literal form is a guaranteed false negative, which is what the first check did. 57 new BN/EN pairs. **STILL NOT DONE:** MR-6b the release/re-release notifications to guardians (the 7 `monthly_report.*` templates are registered and unused), MR-7 the PDF + print queue + the Principal's class roll-up. **Still never driven in a running app, and no report has yet been generated against real data.** GEMINI_API_KEY is now set locally and on both dev + prod (`/opt/scdhub/{dev,prod}/.env`, services restarted, health 200) but **nothing has called Gemini yet**. **Now / next:** MR-6b, MR-7, then a live drive on the dev site before the PR into `dev`.

- 2026-08-01: **BUILT — Monthly Progress Report MR-1..MR-4 + the GraphQL surface, branch `feat/monthly-report` (off `dev`). Server only; NO UI yet.** Built straight through the slices in order after the PRD landed. **MR-1 metric core:** `MonthlyMetricsService` windows the SP-1/SP-2 reads to a calendar month and adds the four planes the profile never reported (Hifz, library, guardian participation, fees paid), plus coverage and the cohort. The rules are pinned by test, not comment — submission is measured against **expected-while-present** (`TrackerCounters.received`), a class-test **ABSENT is never a zero**, a sat-but-unmarked exam drops coverage instead, and `classBest` is withheld on a **roster** below 5 rather than on how many children happen to have data. **MR-1 also opened the three models the PRD had named but not read, and two came back different:** `RevisionEntry` stores no grade band (the planning sample's ভালো/মধ্যম split does not exist), and guardian participation has **no read receipt, no inbound reply leg and no meeting attendance** — all dropped, not approximated, and recorded where each metric was specified; `MeetingComment` left the guardian sheet entirely because J-CM8 makes it structurally staff-only. **MR-2 trend rule:** min-sample-in-both-months then a threshold band with `NOT_COMPARABLE` as a real fourth state (a six-day Ramadan month cannot read as −40 pp); `MonthlyReportConfig` is a `key: "SINGLETON"` row with **read-time defaults, never seeded** (D-#97), tolerant of a partial or junk row, and **frozen into every revision** so a released chip stays reproducible after a threshold moves. **MR-3 the document:** a revision is its **own row** (a released one must stay byte-stable while the next is built — the PRD's one-row-per-month line was corrected), a recompute that changes **no printed figure creates nothing**, a DRAFT nobody has seen is amended in place and that **clears the review** because the numbers moved under the text; release refusals live in one pure verdict — `NOT_REVIEWED` has **no override for anyone**, `PROVISIONAL` and `HARD_LOCKED` are Principal-only with a reason, all audited; bulk release under one revocable batch id. **MR-4 the AI seam:** the facts are a **whitelist** built fresh from the snapshot (a field added later cannot leak by default), asserted de-identified **before the first call** so a leak never reaches the provider even once; the model **may not author a number** — every numeral must appear in the facts JSON in either script, one retry, then the MT-1 template fallback, so the report never blocks on an external API; the provider is a one-method seam, v1 Gemini Flash over one fetch, and **no key is needed yet** (absent key = fallback, not an error). **Surface:** release console, draft/review/release/bulk-release/revoke/config mutations, and the guardian read (RELEASED revisions of own child only); a narrowed subject teacher gets stripped per-subject rows in **both** this month and last month's comparison block, no fee block, and **no AI paragraph at all**. Contract: `report:release` synced three places + BN/EN labels, 7 `monthly_report.*` template keys, 6 `MONTHLY_REPORT_*` audit kinds. Shipped as its own commit first: the **`·` → .notdef fix** in `pdfRenderer.strongFont` — the live student-profile and English-Drive PDFs had been printing empty boxes wherever they wrote that separator. Gates GREEN (executed): shared build; server typecheck; vocab verifier PASS; **full server jest 145 suites / 2449 tests** (+108 new, was 2341 at the branch point); the D-#387 registry guard caught the three new error classes and they are registered as expected-not-fault. **NOT DONE:** MR-5b the staff UI screens (console, comment review, bulk release), MR-6 the release/re-release notifications + the nightly `/triggers` sweep endpoint (`sweepSectionMonth` exists and is tested; **nothing calls it yet**), MR-7 the PDF + print queue + the Principal's class roll-up. **Nothing has been driven in a running app and no report has ever been generated against real data** — the executed-verification gate is met for the unit rules only. **Now / next:** MR-5b, then MR-6, then MR-7; then a live drive on the dev site before any PR into `dev`.

- 2026-07-31: **PLANNED — Support-Book production module (D-#403–#413), build contract `docs/prd-support-book.md`, slices SB-1..SB-6. Docs-only; nothing built.** Owner brought `studybook-pipeline` (the standalone CLI that produced the C1-BAN সহায়িকা: 54 lessons, 493 blocks, 201 image slots, both print editions rendered) and asked whether the whole production process could move in here as a **team** workflow — author, image generator, reviewer, senior reviewer, book generator — with every message and reason for change retrievable afterwards. Pre-flight read the pipeline end to end (`validate-studybook.js`, `build-book.js`, `src/lib/*`, `src/tools/preview.js`) plus its governance set (README v2.2, SCHEMA v1.3, REF-1, ASSEMBLY, PROJECT-INSTRUCTIONS) **and** the matching seams here, and the finding that shaped the whole PRD is that **~60 % of the hard infrastructure already exists**: Drive-backed file storage (`DriveStore` + `StoredFile` + `GET /files/:id`, D-#70) answers the image-store-with-tags requirement outright; AC-1's role-as-template + per-user grants (D-#193/#212) answers the new-roles requirement without touching `ROLES`; `ReviewAssignment` supplies a proven round/verdict shape to copy; `docxConvert` is the precedent for shelling out to a heavy binary. **Genuinely missing: an LLM integration (there is none anywhere in this repo — the two "Claude" hits in `server/` are comments telling an admin to copy text into Claude Desktop) and a Chromium render worker.** The load-bearing rulings: **D-#403** the AGENTS scope boundary is honoured by storing the programme's policy as versioned `PolicyDoc` DATA rather than repo files — the first framing ("policy stays upstream") was wrong because the chat cannot emit a valid lesson without REF-1/REF-2/README §4/SCHEMA/letter-inventory, and this repo already imports curriculum as rows rather than files (ADR-006); every generation stamps a `policySetHash`, which both answers "why does this read this way" years later and is exactly what makes a ~20k-token prefix cache. **D-#404** the book plane gets its own Mongo connection (owner asked; it also makes the isolation structural, the ADR-005 posture) — no cross-connection joins, capacity irrelevant since images live in Drive. **D-#405** five roles = seven grantable `book:*` permissions, none RESERVED, and the vocab rides `ROUTINE_SUBJECTS` + `ROSTER_CLASS_LEVELS` so ISLAM/Nursery are expressible and **no wire contract moves** (the D-#392 lesson applied before the fact). **D-#406** Mongo authoritative per-lesson, `book.json` materialized at build, export-to-zip keeps the CLI runnable. **D-#407** the renderer is vendored into `/book-pipeline/` and spawned, never ported (ASSEMBLY §1: the frozen core is never modified), on a separate worker process so a Chromium OOM cannot take down attendance. **D-#408** both authoring paths share one patch shape and one validator; **D-#410** an escalation is item-anchored, multi-round, and its resolution produces a citing patch rather than mutating a lesson; **D-#411** two logs — `Audit` for who-did-what, `BookEvent` for why; **D-#412** the LLM chat is built LAST because the Desktop path already works. *Process note:* the D-# range was reserved only after re-reading the live `DECISIONS.md` — highest row was **D-#402**, appended by a concurrent session while this one was open (AGENTS rule 3 / the parallel-worktree hazard, caught again, same as the exams and monthly-report PRDs). Nothing here touched code, so no verifier was run. **Hosting settled the same session (D-#413), by measurement rather than assumption:** owner ruled the book DB lives on the VM, so the live host was read read-only — 4 OCPU / **23 GB with 1.4 GB in use, load average 0.00 after 48 days uptime**, 86 GB free of 96 GB, both app servers together ~300 MB. Steady-state addition ~350 MB, render peak 2–4 GB for minutes against 22 GB available: **the machine is 94 % idle and nowhere near a free-tier ceiling** (96 GB of the 200 GB block-storage limit; egress irrelevant). **A second VM is refused** — this instance already consumes the entire Always Free ARM allocation, so another would bill on the PAYG account to relieve a host doing nothing. Three constraints found in the same pass and pinned into SB-1/SB-4 because each is a default that bites: **`mongod` must have its WiredTiger cache capped at 1 GB** (default is ~11 GB here, for a database under 100 MB); the host is **`aarch64`, where Puppeteer publishes no bundled Chromium at all**, so it needs the OS chromium snap plus `PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH` — an env var, which is what lets D-#407's byte-identical vendored renderer stand — verified under the systemd unit, not just an interactive shell; and **build concurrency 1**, because the VM has **no swap**, so real pressure means an OOM kill rather than a slowdown. Already installed, not to be redone: `pdffonts` (the post-render font audit) and `soffice`; `sharp` has arm64 prebuilds. Only Chromium needs installing. **Now / next:** the owner answers the remaining prd §8 questions (which book is next, who the senior reviewer is, LLM spend ceiling, Drive retention, whether the image strip chain stays local), then SB-1 (policy store + book/lesson models + ported validator + merge, server only) on its own branch off `dev`. **Note on this working tree:** these docs were written on `fix/guardian-row-overflow`, which is carrying its own uncommitted STATUS/CHANGELOG edits — the support-book docs want their own commit, not that branch's.

- 2026-07-30 (cont. 3): **FIXED — two guardian-facing rows that squeezed themselves off the screen (owner report, two screenshots), branch `fix/guardian-row-overflow`.** One root cause behind both: React Native defaults `flexShrink` to **0** where CSS defaults it to 1, so in a row with a single `flexShrink: 1` child that child absorbs 100% of the overflow. (1) `ChildHomeworkScreen` + `GuardianHomeScreen` render the parent-facing homework status — deliberately a SENTENCE, not a chip word — in a `Badge` beside the subject title. The badge never yielded, so the title column collapsed and `ইংরেজি` wrapped as `ইংরে/জি` with `HW-C1-ENG-0013` one character per line. The sentence moved to its own full-width line beneath the title; the short RESUBMIT chip stays inline. (2) `CommentEntryScreen`: the attachment row had no `flexWrap` and `Button` has fixed padding with no `flexShrink`, so label + "Open attachment" + "Remove" overflowed a phone width and Remove ran off the edge — the row wraps now, the label shrinks first, and `marginLeft: "auto"` right-aligns the buttons in place of a `flex: 1` spacer that just collapsed. Only the two rows carrying a sentence-length badge were touched; the other five `flexShrink: 1` rows in GuardianHomeScreen pair with short badges and were left alone. The shared `Badge` was deliberately NOT given a global `flexShrink` — it would fix the whole class at once but changes every badge row in the app, which cannot be eyeballed in one session. Gates: app typecheck clean, expo web export exit 0 (layout-only; no server contract touched, so no jest/vocab run applies). **Still owed:** a look at the two screens on a phone-width viewport. **Now / next:** PR into dev.

- 2026-07-30 (cont. 2): **BUILT — a person can name the class of an old print (D-#392, PQ-9), branch `feat/print-tag-class`.** Owner ask after D-#391 shipped: "how can we fix no class items and tag appropriately". Every automatic route was measured against the live 72 first and each fails: the 18 **link** jobs are opaque `docs.google.com/document/d/…` ids and none of those URLs appears on a job that DOES name a class, so there is nothing to join to; **requester scope** disambiguates nothing, since no teacher in the roster teaches only one class (and the owner account files 24 of the 72 with no teaching grant at all); and the **file-name codes**, which do read on 42 of 72, name a class the requester does not teach for 12 of the 22 rows where that could be cross-checked. So the code became a **suggestion** and a human confirms it. Shipped: a class + optional-subject picker on any untagged history row, gated to the Office or the teacher who filed it, applied to **every job behind the row** (the row is a document — tagging one print would split it) and **all-or-nothing** so a group never half-lands; a class-test job refused outright because its class belongs to the exam record it mirrors; both sides audited (`PRINT_REQUEST_CLASS_TAGGED`: before, after, byOffice); omit-leaves / null-clears so a wrong tag is correctable. Pre-fill measured by running the SHIPPED parser over the live rows: **42 of 72 arrive with a class, 20 of those with a subject too**, 30 typed from scratch; `suggestionEvidence` shows the exact filename it read. Trap recorded: `_` is a regex word character, so `\bEng\b` never matches inside `C1_Eng_Block02` — the dominant shape here — and both readers normalize separators first (a test caught it, not review). Gates: server+app typecheck clean, vocab verifier PASS, jest **2341/2341** (140 suites, 16 new), expo web export exit 0. Diag kept (read-only, untracked): `diag-print-noclass-recovery.ts` (scores each recovery signal), `diag-print-suggest-verify.ts` (runs the real parser over the live rows). **Still owed:** a click-through on the dev site — nothing in PQ-7/8/9 has been driven in the running app yet. **Now / next:** PR into dev, then promote.

D-#390 had fixed the axis (roster-derived chips) but not what the rows answer — the class axis read `classId`, set by `ClassTestService` alone (25 of 269 live jobs). The history now reads the **effective class**: `classId ?? copiesClassId`, the latter being the class a CLASS_PRESENT job prints one copy per present student of (D-#294) — a class the teacher explicitly picked, so this is a field the job already carries, not an inference. Own `classId` wins where both are set (one-directional: the fallback branch is guarded by `classId: null`); the effective class joins the grouping key; rows expose `classId`/`classLevel` separate from `latest.classId`, which keeps meaning the job field. New `noClass` filter selects the honest 72-document remainder, still deliberately untagged. Gates: server+app typecheck clean, vocab verifier PASS, jest **2325/2325** (140 suites, 8 new), expo web export exit 0. Diag kept (read-only, untracked): `diag-print-class-tagging.ts`, `diag-print-class-fallback.ts`, `diag-print-effective-verify.ts` (the last runs the real service and prints the per-chip counts). **Still owed:** a click-through on the dev site — no screen of this has been driven yet. **Now / next:** PR into dev, then promote.

- 2026-07-30: **SHIPPED TO PROD — reprint-history filters + the silent-truncation fix (D-#390, PQ-7), PR #431 → dev, PR #432 → main, prod deploy green.** Owner report off the live screen, four complaints, one prod count settling all of them: 269 printed jobs → 263 grouped documents against a **default page limit of 200**, cut by `rows.slice(0, limit)` with no flag — that is the "not all files are coming". PQ6.8 had made the 1000-doc SCAN cap honest and left the page limit silent; at 306 total jobs the scan flag never fires, so a 200-row list read as the whole history. Added: printed-on window (`fromKey`/`toKey`, applied to the jobs before grouping, with the `printedAt`→`requestedAt` fallback the grouping already uses) and an Office-only requester filter on new `requesterIds`. Fell out while reading the screen: the "no class" chip shared the ANY sentinel with "all", so it was permanently lit and filtered nothing. The 244 untagged rows are left alone by decision — class is not inferable from a title, and they are now reachable by teacher + date. Gates: server+app typecheck clean, vocab verifier PASS, jest **2317/2317** (140 suites, 6 new), expo web export exit 0, 9 new BN strings verified \u-escaped in the bundle. **Not yet driven in the running app** — the filters want a click-through before the PR. Diag kept: `server/scripts/diag-print-history.ts` (read-only; prints status/class/requester/grouping counts per DB).

- 2026-07-30 (cont. 3): **PLANNED — Monthly Progress Report (D-#393–#402), build contract `docs/prd-monthly-report.md`, slices MR-1..MR-7. Docs-only; nothing built.** Owner ask: a per-student report generated at month end from available data, updated as late data lands, released by Principal/Office individually or in bulk, re-released when it changes, with AI-generated guardian comments reviewed by a human. **Pre-flight finding that shaped the whole thing:** the metrics are ~90 % already computed — `StudentProfileService` (per-subject HW/AS tallies incl. CORRECT/PARTIAL/WRONG, resubmission-is-one-sheet, absent-non-submission), `StudentProfileContextService` (`absentUncoveredDays`, `absentStreakMax`, monthly series, comment tallies), `ClassTestSummaryService.studentProfile`, `WholePictureService.attendanceSplitOf` — so this is a **snapshot + release + AI layer over existing reads**: one collection (`MonthlyReport`), one singleton config, one permission (`report:release`, contract sync), everything else derived (D-#85). The load-bearing rulings: **D-#393** a released revision is IMMUTABLE — late data raises revision N+1 in DRAFT and the guardian keeps seeing N until someone releases it (the CM-1 sealed-on-delivery posture), and a recompute that changes no reported number raises nothing; **D-#394** every metric carries denominator + coverage + as-of, school days are DERIVED from attendance (so Ramadan/holidays need no calendar), submission rates use expected-while-present, and release is BLOCKED below 80 % coverage with a Principal-only override — this is the answer to "on the 30th the class test isn't checked yet"; **D-#395** a trend is min-sample-in-both-months + a threshold band with `NOT_COMPARABLE` as a real state, thresholds Principal-tunable via a `key: "SINGLETON"` config with read-time defaults (D-#97) and **frozen into each revision**; **D-#396** comparators are numbers only (class avg + class best + a school-best attendance figure), class best suppressed below a 5-student section; **D-#399** ONE AI paragraph over a de-identified facts JSON with a validator that rejects any numeral the model invented, template fallback, human review before release, Gemini Flash behind a provider-agnostic seam. Also pinned: complaint = CONCERN comments only (D-#400); fees are PAYMENTS ONLY, no dues, because no fee-schedule model exists and finance stores no balances (D-#401); all classes incl. Nursery/KG, vocab out of v1, exam marks LINKED not duplicated, and no "late" metric because the school does not admit late students (D-#402). **A full sample sheet with fake data was rendered through the live pdfkit + NotoSansBengali engine and reviewed by the owner BEFORE the PRD was written** (3 A4 pages, ≤7-column tables, scenarios chosen to exercise each rule) — which surfaced a real defect: **`·` (U+00B7) renders as .notdef boxes** in that engine, and `StudentProfileSheetService` + `englishDrivePdf.ts` use it as a separator today, so the LIVE student-profile and English-Drive PDFs are printing boxes; the one-line fix in `pdfRenderer.strongFont()` is owed as its own change. *Process note:* the PRD's D-# range was reserved only after re-reading the live `DECISIONS.md` (highest row **D-#392**, appended by a concurrent session while this one was open) — AGENTS rule 3 / the parallel-worktree hazard, caught again. Nothing here touched code, so no verifier was run. **Now / next:** the `·` fix as a standalone change, then MR-1 (snapshot compute, server only) on its own branch off `dev`. Open questions for the owner in prd §13 (bilingual sheet, shorter Nursery/KG sheet, class average for tiny cohorts, a fee-schedule model later, who gets the class roll-up).

- 2026-07-28: **PLANNED — Exams, script custody chain & report cards (D-#375–#382), build contract `docs/prd-exams.md`, slices EX-1..EX-10. Docs-only; nothing built.** Owner ask with real evidence: six scanned class bundles (CT/Adab grids + per-subject checker/rechecker mark sheets) and five generated report-card bundles for session 2026, exam "Half Yearly-Sylhet". Two halves: (1) **marks → report card** — the numbers the school already prints, computed instead of hand-totalled; (2) the **custody chain** the owner actually asked for — every physical handover of question papers / answer scripts / mark sheets is a two-signature event with a count (giver creates → only the named receiver acknowledges; a count mismatch becomes `DISPUTED` carrying BOTH numbers, never silently resolved), across 13 stages from question issue to archive, with derived reconciliation (`issued = used + unused returned`) that **blocks tabulation** while unbalanced or disputed (D-#382). Arithmetic pinned off the generated cards and verified against them: nearest-0.5 scale conversion via one shared helper, `obtained = Σ components`, GPA = mean of subject points (2 dp), **any single F ⇒ overall 0.00/F** (Rehana prints 0.00 F at 552/800 — no total-based rule reproduces that), `Highest Marks` = cohort max derived at render, absence recorded per COMPONENT not per student (D-#377). **The load-bearing modelling ruling is D-#376: component composition is PER PAPER, not per class band** — Class-3 Maths genuinely had no CT (a per-band lookup would have written a silent 0 into ~16 students' cards and dropped each a grade band), and Nursery/KG share a subject list but not a shape; three shapes ship day one (1/2/3 components). Reuses rather than rebuilds: `PrintRequest` (D-#281/PQ) for printing, the CT-8/CO-8 submit→approve→publish gate, the class-test tracker as the CT source (both `MEAN` and `BEST_N` modes, D-#378), `Section`/`ExamPaper.sectionId` so real sections need no migration later (D-#379). Backfill = the 70 students on the 2026 cards (KG..Five; no Nursery), read from the GENERATED cards not the handwritten sheets, strict diff with no tolerances as the grading engine's regression test, halting on the two known identity defects in the source (duplicate `schoolId` 0073 → Barakah is `0079`; three struck-out KG rows) (D-#381). All 8 owner questions ratified in the same session (PRD §9) — **no open blockers**. *Process note:* the PRD first reserved D-#369–#378, but a concurrent session had landed D-#369–#374; re-checked the live log before appending and renumbered to D-#375–#382 (AGENTS rule 3 / parallel-worktree hazard, caught in practice). **Now / next:** build EX-1 (exam + paper + grade scale) on its own branch off `dev`; nothing in this session touched code, so no verifier was run.

- 2026-07-26 (cont. 2): **BUILT — observation prior-focus carry-forward (CO-10) + the observer's own review history (CO-11), D-#363, branch `feat/observation-prior-focus`.** Owner finding: the review form asks "did the prior focus progress?" (পূর্ববর্তী ফোকাসের অগ্রগতি) while showing NOTHING about the prior observation, so a reviewer working through several teachers answers from memory — and the only prior link, `prevObservationId`, is written solely by `requestReReview` (same session), so a normal later observation of the same teacher had no link at all. **CO-10:** new `observationPriorFocusContext` resolves `prevObservationId` when set, else the newest settled REF-11 row of the same teacher before this one with **same-subject preferred outright** over merely more recent; the review form quotes that focus (date · subject, an other-subject or re-review prior badged as such) above the REF-11 block, and when there is NO prior the progress select and the new note are **not rendered at all** instead of inviting a meaningless answer. **The visibility call is the sensitive part:** the prior review is usually another observer's row, which `canReadObservation` does not expose, so the read returns a **fixed narrow slice — classDate, subject, form, growthFocus, oneStrength, priorFocusProgress + sameSubject/isReReview — and NEVER the prior domains, gates, teacher response, fairness rating or the prior observer's identity**; a test pins the exact key set by name so a later "just add domains" fails loudly. Gated to the row's assigned observer or `observation:manage` — the observed teacher has no path. New optional `priorFocusNote` free text beside the enum (REF-11 only, trimmed-or-null). **CO-11:** new `myObservationReviews` reuses the oversight filter/paging engine with `observerId` **forced server-side** (no such argument exists, so it cannot be pointed at a peer) and no state restriction — reviews stop vanishing the moment they are submitted; new `MyReviewHistoryScreen` + the filter block extracted to a shared `ObservationFilters` so both screens filter identically, and rows open the existing detail screen which already embeds the footage. `sectionId` (class) added as a filter on both — the owner asked for it and no screen had it although every row stores one. Gate GREEN (executed): vocab verifier PASS; server typecheck; app typecheck; **server jest 137/137 suites, 2269/2269 tests** (+19 new); **expo web export exit 0 with the full log inspected** (per the export-exit-code-mask trap). **STILL OWED before promotion:** a live drive on the dev site — as the assigned observer (prior card appears / first-ever observation hides both fields / the note round-trips) and through the history screen's class+subject+date filters. **Next:** PR into dev, drive it on the dev site, then promote.

Single-date `dayPart` + period count on `StaffLeaveApplication`; the missed periods are resolved at apply time (early leave anchors to the teacher's own last teaching period — nursery 6 vs class 1–5 8, D-#57) and stored, so the fan-out covers those meetings only. `userIdsOnLeave` is period-scoped now, so a late-entry teacher still ranks as available for period 6. Partial days deliberately do NOT drive the ✘→LEAVE attendance overlay. - 2026-07-23 (cont. 24): **FIXED - Assignment declare pending report showed the internal continuous week number ("Week 3") instead of the delivery-month assignment label.** Owner prod finding: the group header read `Week 3 - 2026-07-19` while every row delivered on `2026-07-23`. - 2026-07-23 (cont. 23): **FIXED — "Assignment declare pending" was blind on the delivery day + Today prep card now names what to prepare (D-#354, owner findings), branch `feat/assignment-edit` (same branch, follow-up commits).** Owner: the report said "Nothing under these filters" while the Assignments screen showed Nursery—Arabic "Not delivered" for the week delivering that same day. **Root cause:** `expectedItemsForWeek` returns `dateOnlyISO()` (a FULL instant) but `asNotDeclaredRows` compared it to a date key — `"2026-07-23T00:00:00.000Z" > "2026-07-23"` is TRUE (longer string, equal prefix) — so the whole week was skipped as "not late yet" on its own delivery day and only appeared the NEXT day. Narrowed to a key before comparing (the `.slice(0,10)` `assignmentWork` already used); `deliveryDateKey` now carries a real key, not a raw instant. Owner ratified the semantics: **the delivery day counts** (pending/to-do list, matching the screen); only the future stays silent. The old tests missed it because they mocked `deliveryDate` as a bare key — 2 new regression tests pin the REAL ISO format. **Second ask:** the Today gold prep card showed only a countdown, so a teacher couldn't tell WHAT to make — `AssignmentPrep` gains `cells[] {classLevel, subject, sectionId}` (free: the rotation walk already had them), rendered as "শ্রেণি ২ · গণিত" (capped at 4, then "+N আরও"); also dropped the "বাকি" suffix once the countdown expires (it read "সময় শেষ বাকি"). Gate GREEN (executed): server+app tsc; reconReport jest 19/19 (+2); **verified on prod data — the Today range went 0 → 11 rows incl. the reported `cls-1 ARABIC`, and prep cells resolved per teacher (Maruf cls4/MATH, Nuha cls4/SCI, Shah Mahfuj cls2/MATH+cls5/BAN)**; UI screenshot shows the gold card reading "শ্রেণি ২ · গণিত". Probe residue: a teacher password was reset in **scdhub_local only** (test copy) to drive the UI. **Next:** PR into dev, then promote.
- 2026-07-23 (cont. 23): **BUILT — tracker REVERTs are now audited (D-#354), branch `feat/revert-audit` (isolated worktree `rev-aud` off dev) → PR into dev.** Closes the observability hole found while investigating the owner's "Fida marked all Nursery homework submitted + checked but the report shows pending" report. That report was proven CORRECT (probe reproduced the card exactly: 173 given / 83 returned / 87 awaiting submission = 40 untouched + 21 he chased himself + 25 absentees he re-delivered + 1), but the investigation could not be *closed*, because `popActionGroup` (D-#338) **deletes** the popped `stateDates` stamps — so a reverted record is byte-identical to one never worked on, and the audit log had NO homework/assignment lifecycle event (`HW_FILE_ATTACHED` was the only HW kind). "Did someone undo the teacher's work?" was unfalsifiable. Now `HW_RECORD_REVERTED` / `AS_RECORD_REVERTED` are written by both revert services after the splice, carrying item + student, `revertedFrom`→`restoredTo`, admin flag, and the **full popped stamp list (state/at/by)**. Scoped to revert ONLY — forward moves already self-document in `stateDates`, and per-transition rows would balloon (21 per bulk mark). No app change needed: the Audit Log screen builds its kind filter from returned rows and already renders `metaJson`, so it surfaces end-to-end. Gate GREEN (executed): shared build; server tsc; revert suites 23/23; **full server jest 2099/2099 (127 suites, +2 audit-trace tests)**. App untouched (server-only). **Next:** owner reviews, then promote. Note: D-#353 was taken by the concurrent assignment-edit session, so this took D-#354.

- 2026-07-12 (later): **OBSERVABILITY HARDENED (D-#286) — the follow-up to the D-#284/#285 incident. All three open items closed, each VERIFIED, not assumed.** **(1) The error dashboard is fast:** **https://scdhub-errors.shafayet.me** (login `scdsylhet2022@gmail.com`; password in `/opt/glitchtip/admin-credentials.txt`). It was slow because it went DIRECT to the VM while prod is Cloudflare-proxied — and it could not be proxied because **Cloudflare Universal SSL covers only ONE label deep** (`*.shafayet.me` matches `scdhub.shafayet.me`, NOT `errors.scdhub.shafayet.me`). Renamed to a single-label host + orange-clouded: **TLS 2.1 s → 0.04 s, load 5.5 s → 0.11 s (~35x)**. Caddy took an LE cert THROUGH Cloudflare (ACME challenge served to Cloudflare IPs). Old vhost retired; **the old `errors.scdhub` DNS record is still in Cloudflare and can be deleted**. **(2) Alerting works and was PROVEN by a delivered email:** GlitchTip was on `consolemail://` — it printed mail to the container log and delivered NOTHING, so an alert rule would have notified nobody. Now Gmail SMTP (app password, VM-only; port 465 ⇒ `smtp+ssl`, NOT `smtp+tls`), an **`Any new error`** rule on every project; triggered a real fault and confirmed the alert fired (notification row + no SMTP error). Recipients = GlitchTip USERS on the team — currently `scdsylhet2022@gmail.com` only; a 2nd address needs an ACCOUNT, which also grants dashboard access (it carries user ids/roles), so it was NOT granted. **(3) Dev captures to its OWN project** (`scdhub-server-dev`, id 3, `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT=development`) with **NO alert rule on purpose** — dev noise must never train anyone to ignore a prod alert. Verified via the built-in `SENTRY_DEBUG_ROUTE` (enabled → captured → **removed again**; prod correctly refuses that route at all). **Gotcha now recorded:** a GlitchTip DSN key is the project UUID with **dashes stripped** — with dashes the SDK logs `Invalid Sentry Dsn` ONCE and then drops every event in silence (this cost two restarts). **All VM edits are backed up** (`.env`, `compose.yml`, `Caddyfile` → `.bak-*`). **Prod/dev/errors all healthy (200).** **Open:** delete the stale `errors.scdhub` DNS record; the app project (`scdhub-app`) has an alert rule but the Expo client never sends events; Lesson-Plans response is still ~0.5 MB on the wire (paginate the tree).

- 2026-07-12: **PROD INCIDENT — Lesson Plans was down (intermittently, for everyone); root-caused, hotfixed, LIVE (D-#284). And the reason nobody knew: error tracking was never connected (D-#285).** **The bug:** `contentTree` sorted IN MONGO on `envelopeJson.payload.session_plan.period_index` — a path inside a **Mixed** blob, which **no index can serve** — so Mongo sorted the **FULL documents** in memory. The query also pulled `renderedMarkdown` + the entire import envelope per artifact: **23.6 MB / 6,591 rows (~29 MB BSON)** against MongoDB's **32 MB** in-memory-sort ceiling → `MongoServerError: Sort exceeded memory limit of 33554432 bytes`. Sitting ON the line is why it was **intermittent** and read as flakiness. **NOT caused by the release** — the content module was untouched by all 35 promoted commits; **the collection GREW past the limit** (prod 6,591 current artifacts vs local 1,501 — the bug lived in the gap between test data and real data, which is exactly why 4 testing rounds missed it). **Fix (PR #191 → #192, live on `main`):** project the two bulk reads to the ~12 fields they use (`artifact(id:)` still reads the full doc for the plan view, ADR-006) and sort in JS via `compareForTree` (ordering pinned by +8 tests — it was the ONLY reason the Mongo sort existed). Rejected `allowDiskUse` (raises the threshold, keeps the query slow). **Measured on prod: 23.6 MB → 1.80 MB; 7.5 s → 0.35 s (from the VM); zero sort errors since.** **The deeper miss (D-#285):** MON-2 wired the Sentry SDK but **`SENTRY_DSN` was never set on the prod host**, and the code no-ops without it — so the server **silently discarded every fault since first deploy**. GlitchTip held ONE issue (the 2026-06-15 setup smoke test) and sat blind through an outage firing every few minutes; the stack trace that solved this in seconds was never captured. DSN now set + verified by a real captured fault (**gotcha: a GlitchTip DSN key is the project UUID with DASHES STRIPPED** — the SDK rejects dashes with `Invalid Sentry Dsn`, and then drops events silently). GlitchTip itself was never broken: from the VM it answers in **3 ms**; the VM is healthy (23 GB RAM, 1.4 GB used). **STILL OPEN:** (1) **`errors.scdhub.shafayet.me` is slow/stalls from outside** — it is grey-clouded (DNS-only) while prod is Cloudflare-proxied (D-#283); it can't be proxied as-is because Cloudflare Universal SSL covers only ONE label deep — **rename to a single-label host (e.g. `scdhub-errors.shafayet.me`) and orange-cloud it**. (2) No **alerting** on new GlitchTip issues — capture without notification just moves the blindness. (3) The Lesson-Plans **response** is still ~0.5 MB on the wire (paginate the tree). (4) The **dev** service has no `SENTRY_DSN` either.

(2) **Open gave no feedback** — a set/plan PDF renders on demand and an upload streams through the server, so it looked dead and a double-tap opened duplicate tabs → reused the existing **`useFileOpen`** hook (BUG-014), which was already in the repo but never wired to the print screen; mandatory is enforced at the FORM, the same seam `createPrintRequest` uses. **Gate GREEN (executed):** shared build, server+app tsc 0 errors, vocab verifier PASS, **jest 1779/1779 (106 suites)** — +2 asserting the teacher's colour/sides choice actually REACHES the queue row (the thing that would silently regress) and that omitting them still lands on the defaults. **`dev` is now 35 commits ahead of `main`.** **⚠️ THE PROD MIGRATION IS NOT RUN.** The dev DB was migrated; **production has its own database and has NOT been.** Before/at the prod deploy: `npx tsx server/scripts/migrate-classtest-print-requests.ts --commit` — without it **every class test created before PQ-5 disappears from the Office's prod queue.** Idempotent. **Next:** the deliberate `dev → main` promotion PR (awaiting the owner's go-ahead) + that migration against prod.

- 2026-07-12: **INFRA — "prod is slow" fixed live: Cloudflare in front of prod + immutable asset caching in Caddy (D-#283). NO repo code change; nothing to promote.** Diagnosis was measured, not guessed: the server answered in ~100 ms but the direct BD→Oracle route ran at ~18–45 KB/s with ~20% SYN-retransmit connects, and the 4.4 MB (1.1 MB gzip) web bundle re-downloaded every visit (Caddy sent no `Cache-Control`). Fixes, both executed + verified this session: (a) VM Caddyfile — `immutable` on `/_expo/static/*`+`/assets/*`, `no-cache` on HTML (deploys propagate instantly, no CF purge ever needed), API routes untouched, backup beside it; (b) `shafayet.me` DNS moved to Cloudflare (registrar chain: BestWebHostBD reseller → Spaceship, Inc.; NS kenneth/venus.ns.cloudflare.com) — ONLY prod `scdhub` proxied; `dev.scdhub`/`errors.scdhub` DNS-only (free cert covers ONE subdomain level — never orange-cloud a 2-level name); mail/cPanel records all DNS-only, mail untouched; SSL **Full (strict)**; **Always-Use-HTTPS OFF on purpose** (Caddy already 308s; the CF redirect would break LE HTTP-01 renewal — do NOT re-enable). **Measured result: bundle fetch 29 s → <1 s (`Cf-Cache-Status: HIT`); prod+dev `/healthz` green; dev basic-auth intact.** Follow-up (backlog, not urgent): shrink the 4.4 MB bundle (source-map-explorer + lazy-loaded screens).

Idempotent. **Testing guide `docs/testing-guide-2026-07-10.md` updated to round 3** (all PRs on `dev` → test the dev site, no local checkout; migration promoted to required; new **Part D** for the round-2 fixes). **Production (`main`) is still at PR #182 — none of this is live; `dev` is ~18 commits ahead.** **Next:** user runs the migration → tests Parts B/C/D on the dev site → then the deliberate `dev → main` promotion PR. *(Dropped by user decision: a Quran option in Admin → Proxy grants — a `ScopeGrant` is keyed class+section+subject and a Quran group has no `classId`, so that form structurally cannot name one; cover management already hands over the Quran marking duty.)*

- 2026-07-10: **BUILT — Print queue PQ-5 (D-#281): class-test printing absorbed + delivered notification + migration.** Branch `feat/print-queue-absorb` (stacked on `feat/print-queue`/PR #185). `ClassTest.printRequestId` ⇄ `PrintRequest.classTestId`: filing a class test now also files its queue row, so the Office works **ONE** list. Transitions mirror **both ways** — advancing the queue row runs `mirrorToClassTest` (status + its own `CLASS_TEST_PRINTED`/`CANCELLED` audit), and the legacy `markClassTestPrinted` writes back onto the row — each guarded on `status:"REQUESTED"` so a mirrored write can never double-apply. The class test keeps its own lifecycle (its `PRINTED` status is what makes it the official exam, CT-1). A `trusted` flag lets the internal caller skip `assertSourceResolves`, because a class-test paper is a `classtest_question` StoredFile, not a `print_upload`; never settable from GraphQL. **`PRINT_DELIVERED`** notification added (kind + BN/EN labels + `print.delivered.{title,body}` templates + dedupeKey + `emitPrintDelivered`), fired best-effort from `markDelivered` so it can never block the transition — contract synced in **all three places** (vocab, labels, verifier), which caught two omissions. **`ClassTestPrintQueueScreen` retired** (route, import, and the three dead app ops removed); CT home now crosses to the Print tab. **Migration:** `server/scripts/migrate-classtest-print-requests.ts` — dry-run by default, idempotent (repairs a half-run's missing back-link), status carried 1:1; nothing is back-filled as DELIVERED because that state never existed, so printed-but-uncollected jobs land in "Printing done", which is where they belong. **Run it with `--commit` BEFORE a PQ-5 server serves the queue**, or pre-existing class tests vanish from the Office's view.
- 2026-07-10: **BUILT — the three deferred follow-ups (D-#282).** Three branches off `dev`, one PR each. **Guardian class-note attachments** (`feat/guardian-classnote-attachments`): the file carries no back-ref to a student, so `assertClassNoteFileReadAccess` reverse-resolves the owning `ClassNote` by `attachmentIds` (new sparse multikey index) and checks an ACTIVE linked child in that section/subject-group; orphan uploads and revoked links both fail closed. `childClassNotes` now returns `attachments{id,name,mime}` batched; the guardian screen taps to open. **Cross-tracker whole picture** (`feat/cross-tracker-profile`): `wholePicture()` composes class test + homework + assignment + attendance; class-test analytics reused whole and `regressionSlope` exported so attendance shares the ONE trajectory primitive; attendance gains a recent-vs-earlier split; `overall` is conservative (one weak signal is noise — declining needs a down academic trajectory OR two behaviour signals). **Guardian trajectory** = direction + the child's own numbers in plain Bangla, **no rank, no peer comparison**, built from the same aggregate. **Plan → PDF** (`feat/plan-pdf-print`): the renderer already existed; wired the queue's `openSource` + a plan-viewer "Send to print", and closed a real gap — the Office lacks `content:read`, so `/pdf/artifact/:id` now admits `roster:manage` **only while a live PrintRequest references the artifact**. **Gate GREEN (executed):** server+app tsc, shared-vocab verifier PASS, jest **1765/1765 (105 suites)** — +5 plan-PDF gate, +11 class-note gate, +17 whole-picture, +1 guardian attachments. No new permission; no vocab/contract change; ADR-005 firewall untouched.

- 2026-07-09: **BUILT — Print queue PQ-1..PQ-4 (D-#281).** Branch `feat/print-queue` (off `dev`). ONE queue for everything the Office prints, generalizing the class-test print request. **PQ-1:** `PrintRequest` model (`sourceType` selects EXACTLY ONE of `setId`/`contentArtifactId`/`fileIds[]`/`linkUrl`, enforced in the service AND a model pre-validate hook) + the machine `REQUESTED → PRINTED → DELIVERED` (+`CANCELLED`) — `DELIVERED` is the only genuinely new state; a PRINTED job can never be cancelled. New `PRINT_REQUEST_STATUSES/PURPOSES/SOURCES` vocab + `PRINT_REQUEST_{CREATED,PRINTED,DELIVERED,CANCELLED}` audit kinds. **No new permission** (submit `tracker:write`, operate `roster:manage`). **PQ-2:** `print_upload` StoredFile kind + `POST /files/print` (jpeg/png/pdf ≤10 MB, Drive-first) + a `print_upload` branch in the kind-dispatched `GET /files/:id` gate (uploader or Office only); every source must prove it is printable at request time — a SET must be **ASSEMBLED** (which is exactly why no PDF snapshot is stored), an artifact must be a chapter/session plan, an upload must exist + be a print_upload + belong to the requester, a LINK must parse as absolute http(s). **PQ-3/PQ-4:** one role-aware Print tab — the Office's three buckets with advance actions, the teacher's own requests + New request (upload / link), and "Send to print" on an assembled Set detail (pre-selected source is locked). **Gate GREEN (executed):** shared+server+app tsc, shared-vocab verifier PASS, jest **1726/1726** (+22 printRequest tests: XOR source rule, transition guards, out-of-order rejections, cancel authorization, draft-set/non-plan/upload-ownership rejections). **PQ-5 (absorb the class-test queue + PRINT_DELIVERED notification + migration) NOT BUILT** — it mutates a live feature and needs a migration, so it gets its own PR.
- 2026-07-09: **BUILT — Assignment-prep countdown (D-#280).** Branch `feat/assignment-prep-countdown` (off `dev` after #183 merged). `myDay` gains `assignmentPrep {dueAt, deliveryDateKey, weekNumber, items}`. `PendingAlertService.assignmentWork` now splits the caller's undelivered items at the **deadline INSTANT** — the school's own `dayStartMinutes` (07:00, not hard-coded) on the schedule's **resolved** `deliveryDate`, so a holiday roll carries it. `now < deadline` → amber countdown; `now ≥ deadline` → the existing red `assignment_entry` alert. One lifecycle, **no dead zone**: on delivery-day morning before 07:00 the teacher is still counting down, not overdue. Delivering clears both at once. The app ticks locally (60 s) off the absolute `dueAt`, so remaining time stays truthful without re-querying, and the row sits **above** the red rows (it hasn't slipped yet). **Gate GREEN (executed):** server+app tsc, jest (pendingAlerts rewritten: countdown/overdue split, exact-07:00 flip, custom day-start, delivered-clears-both, last-week-overdue + this-week-countdown coexistence). No vocab/contract change. **Next:** PQ-1 (print queue).
- 2026-07-09: **BUILT — Attendance marker → first class of the day (D-#278) + Today backlog board (D-#279).** Branch `feat/attendance-first-class`, **uncommitted pending local testing**. **D-#278 (AF-1..AF-5):** new `attendanceUnit.ts` — a student is captured in their **first class**: Class 1–5 → their cross-section **Quran group**, Nursery/KG → their **section**. Marker = admin override → **routine first-class teacher** (cover-aware: a `RoutineSubstitution` hands marking to the cover teacher) → **class-teacher fallback**. Reports **roll up unchanged** to class→section (`absenteeReport` / `sectionAbsentees` / `studentAttendanceHistory` / `absentNoApplication` / `unmarkedSections` / guardian) — the group is never a display axis, and even the Quran teacher's roster renders under class/section headings. **No migration** (`StudentAttendanceDay` already had `sectionId` XOR `subjectGroupId`); **no cutover/backfill** (history queries both unit + section, de-duping per day). `SectionAttendanceAssignment` generalized to either unit; new `markAttendanceUnit`/`amendAttendanceUnit`/`assignUnitMarker`/`myMarkingUnits`/`attendanceUnitRoster`/`attendanceUnitDay`. **D-#279:** Today shows RED tappable backlog alerts (attendance / class-note / assignment-**not-delivered**) over a **7-day look-back** with `oldestDateKey`; Principal/Office additionally get a per-class **present/absent** snapshot + the unmarked-sections chase list. Batched: holidays loaded once; `attendanceBacklog.unmarkedMarkingDays` resolves markers purely after ~8 loads. `myMarkingUnits` now returns `[]` on non-FULL days. **Bug caught in build:** `rosterForUnit` returned all Quran-group members, so a Nursery/KG child in a Quran group was markable by that teacher but the roll-up (reading them via their section) would have **silently dropped** the absence — both branches now narrow to students whose resolved unit *is* that unit (regression test added). **Gate GREEN (executed):** server+app tsc, jest **1668+/99 suites** (+`attendanceUnit` 17, +`attendanceRollup` 10, +`pendingAlerts` 17, +D-#278 marker/capture tests). No vocab/contract change, no new permission. **Next:** user local test → commit → PR to dev.
- 2026-07-09: **PLANNED — Attendance marker → first class of the day (D-#278, PRD `docs/prd-attendance-firstclass.md`).** DOCS ONLY, no code yet. Owner AskUserQuestion rulings: Class 1–5 = **per-Quran-group capture + section rollup** (a section's kids span many gender/level-split Quran groups, so no single "first-Quran teacher per section" exists); Nursery/KG = **first-period teacher marks the section**; marker is **routine-derived + cover-aware** (`RoutineSubstitution` cover → cover teacher marks), **fallback = class teacher**, who also keeps all report/history access; **FULL days only** (Saturdays stay attendance-free); **all displays stay class/section-shaped** — the Quran group is a capture+marker unit only. ## Now / next
- **Built (Guardian-engagement reporting, GE-1..GE-3, D-#464/#465)
  [uncommitted, working tree on `dev`]:** owner asked for a report on which guardians
  check what, which screens are most visited, which items go unseen, and who is regular
  vs irregular. **Pre-flight killed three of the four asks:** the guardian portal was a
  PURE READ — no `writeAudit`, no `emit()`, no counter anywhere in
  `GuardianPortalService` — so a family could use the app daily and leave the database
  byte-identical; `CONTENT_READ` has been a declared `AuditEventKind` with **zero
  writers** since ADR-008. Only login regularity was answerable (from `LOGIN_SUCCESS`
  rows with `actorRole: GUARDIAN`). So the instrumentation had to ship before the report
  could mean anything, and both landed together.
  **GE-2 (new signal):** `modules/engagement` + a `guardianviews` collection —
  one row per (guardian × surface × refId × Dhaka day) upserted with `$inc: {count}`,
  **180d TTL**, closed `GUARDIAN_VIEW_SURFACES` vocabulary (unknown surface is dropped,
  never stored). Deliberately NOT the audit log: per-tap rows would outgrow every other
  collection, and ~10 rows per guardian-session would bury the `LOGIN_FAIL` /
  `PERMISSION_DENIED` rows `audits` exists to make findable. Written by
  `recordGuardianView` (guardian taken from the token, never an argument), fired from 9
  guardian surfaces by `useRecordView` — GUARDIAN-only, once per focus not per render,
  fire-and-forget.
  **GE-1/GE-3 (the report):** `guardianEngagement` behind `audit:read` (Principal-only,
  reusing the lever per D-#414 — no new permission, no contract sync). **Three signals
  kept separate, never blended** — they fail independently, so an averaged score would
  hide which one broke. Regularity = **distinct active days**, not raw login count; and
  `NEVER` is banded apart from `LAPSED` (onboarding problem vs retention problem).
  New Principal screen `অভিভাবকদের ব্যবহার` with পরিবার/পর্দা/বিজ্ঞপ্তি tabs,
  least-engaged-first ordering, band/section filters, search.
  **What the live data says (scdhub_local; prod read was blocked, so this is the test
  copy):** 137 active guardians, 131 login-enabled, **only 9 have ever logged in**, 0 in
  the last 7 days, **1 of 228 guardian notifications read**, 0 active push devices. The
  report's first honest finding is that adoption is near zero — which is why the screen
  states contact-only counts and "no view data yet" explicitly rather than letting a
  pre-launch zero read as disengagement.
  **Gate GREEN (executed):** vocab verifier PASS, shared+server+app `tsc` clean, server
  jest **3068/3068 (175 suites)** incl. 20 new engagement tests and the ADR-005 firewall
  test untouched, `expo export --platform web` exit 0. **Verified at the running app:**
  `server/scripts/diag-verify-engagement-e2e.ts` **22/22** (real guardian login, real
  mutation, per-day collapse, unknown-surface rejection, RBAC refusals for
  guardian/anonymous/staff, filter isolation; fixture removed after) and a Playwright
  drive of the screen as Principal **10/10** with no console errors.
  **Now / next:** commit on a branch off `dev` and raise the PR; the report only becomes
  interesting once guardians actually log in, so the open question for the owner is
  whether to chase onboarding (122 never-logged-in families) before reading much into it.

  (no tracker perms) can review; `assertReadSection` stays the gate. Also: `ClassSectionSelect` auto-selects a

  → `{lastTickAt, ageSeconds}`, exposed at **`GET /internal/ticker`** (no PII) for MON-5's off-box monitor.

- **Built (CT-4-FIX — Class Test dashboard/reports RBAC, server, D-#196 [renumbered from #186 at merge — Finance REQ took #186]) [branch `worktree-ct4-rbac-fix`, PR #77 MERGED]:** the pre-existing CT-4 RBAC bug flagged at the CT-5 app review. The four
  CT-4 READ aggregates (`classTestPrincipalDashboard`, `classTestReportsStatus`,
  `classTestClassSubjectAnalysis`, `classTestStudentProfile`) gated `authScopes: { hasPermission:
  "tracker:read" }`, but **OFFICE holds NO `tracker:read`** (it holds `message:dispatch` — which is why
  the overdue-chase already worked) while the §6/§9 + D-#166 intent is for Office to read the dashboard +
  reports. Pothos scope-auth runs `authScopes` BEFORE the resolver, so Office was rejected at the scope
  layer and the intended Principal/Office branch inside `assertDashboardAdmin`/`assertReportRead` was
  unreachable dead code. **Fix (resolver-gating ONLY — NO enum/permission/vocab change):** relax the four
  reads to `authScopes: { authenticated: true }` and let the already-correct gate helpers be the authority
  (the `assertChaseAdmin` pattern) — `assertDashboardAdmin` keeps P/O-only (now reachable by Office);
  `assertReportRead` keeps P/O-unscoped + teacher section-scoped (`assertCanRead`) + denies GUARDIAN/any
  role without `tracker:read`. The chase (`message:dispatch` + P/O) is unchanged. **Non-widening, proven by
  a new schema-execution test `classTestSummaryRbac.test.ts` (11 tests)** that runs real GraphQL queries
  against the built schema with each role's context (exercising the scope-auth layer + the real permission
  map): Office reads all four; a teacher reads only a section it can read and is denied the dashboard;
  GUARDIAN + unauthenticated are denied. **Gate GREEN (executed):** vocab verifier PASS (untouched), shared
  build + shared/server tsc clean, **jest 1090/1090** (65 suites; +1 new suite [11]; the existing
  `classTestSummary.test.ts` stays green). **Server-only.** Not verified live. **Do NOT merge** (PR for
  review).

  deadline derivation is CT-2, not here), `ClassTestSequence` (atomic `CT-C{class}-{SUBJ}-{nnnn}`, the D-#34
  pattern, replacing the fragile composite text key), `ClassTestService` (generateCtId / suggestTestNumber /
  createRequest [POOL_SET set-link OR UPLOADED_PAPER; **year+level+class resolved server-side from the section**,
  D-#143] / markPrinted [REQUESTED→PRINTED + printedAt/By] / cancelRequest [REQUESTED→CANCELLED] / reads),
  `ClassTestFileService` (the uploaded-paper read gate), resolvers (`createClassTestRequest` /
  `suggestClassTestNumber` / `markClassTestPrinted` / `cancelClassTest` / `classTestPrintQueue` / `myClassTests` /
  `classTestsForSection` / `classTest`). **Files (§5.2):** `POST /files/classtest` (multipart, `tracker:write`,
  jpeg/png/pdf ≤ 5 MB reusing `validateUpload`, Drive-first ⇒ 503 + nothing persisted — GP-J8) over the GP-A/M-4
  `DriveStore` `subfolder` (`SCD-Hub-Files/<year>/classtest/`); `GET /files/:id` dispatches `classtest_question`
  to a class-test gate = Office (`roster:manage`) OR the uploading teacher; the Drive id never reaches a client.
  **RBAC (D-#144) — composes existing perms, NO new role/permission (D-#94/#17):** teacher request/results =
  `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite` section verify; Office mark-printed/cancel = `roster:manage`; staff reads =
  `tracker:read`. 3 new audit kinds (CLASS_TEST_REQUESTED/_PRINTED/_CANCELLED). **Firewall:** new class-test block
  (corpus ↛ class-test models + class-test source files ↛ corpus, both ways). **Build rulings:** D-#145 (no
  `schoolId` — single-school convention; renumbered from #142 at merge), D-#143 (year/level/class derived from
  the section — blocks sequence-key spoofing), D-#144 (file-store reuse + Office-or-uploader read gate). **Gate GREEN (executed):**
  vocab verifier PASS, shared build + shared/server tsc clean, **jest 943/943** (56 suites; +1 new suite
  `classTest.test.ts` + 2 new firewall checks over the 910 base; firewall green). **Server-only** (no app — CT-5
  is the app slice; expo export skipped). **Not verified live.** **Next = CT-2** (per-student `ClassTestResult` +
  derived percent/pass-fail + configurable passMark + the school-day-aware exam-date-anchored deadline/overdue).

  COMPLETES the HR module (HR-1..HR-5); it COMPOSES the earlier slices, never twins them. The HR-4
  termination→employmentStatus trigger is its entry point. **New `modules/hr/` model:** `OffboardingCase`
  (trigger + status + lastWorkingDay + embedded clearance checklist + embedded hard-held final settlement +
  exit interview + service-certificate flags). **Services:** `offboardingMath` (pure: trigger→status,
  default checklist, clearanceComplete, lazy date-gate), `OffboardingService` (initiate / clearance / access
  revoke + the `runDueOffboardingRevocations` sweep / settlement compute+release / exit interview /
  certificate / cancel / reads); foundation `ScopeGrantService.revokeAllGrantsForUser` (REUSED).
  **H6.1:** the trigger sets `StaffProfile.employmentStatus` (resignation→resigned, termination→terminated
  [HR-4 entry point], fixed_term_end→contract_ended, retirement→retired — EMPLOYMENT_STATUSES gained the two
  new exit states, D-#117). **H6.2:** configurable clearance checklist (3 §6 categories seeded as read-time
  defaults, D-#97 no-seed). **H6.3 — by the system:** access revoked on the last working day via the EXISTING
  N-2 ticker (no new scheduler; no-cron/lazy posture D-#20/#21) — `runDueOffboardingRevocations` runs
  once-per-day from the tick, disables the `User` login + revokes ALL scope grants (idempotent, lazy
  date-gate); manual admin path too. **H6.4:** `computeFinalSettlement` composes salary pro-rated to last day
  + arrears + full leave encashment (HR-2) − outstanding advance (HR-3, full one_shot netting via the
  payrollMath net-pay guard), **HARD-HELD until clearance complete** (`releaseFinalSettlement`,
  payroll:approve/Principal — throws unless every item done/waived, D-#29; commits advance recovery +
  completes). **H6.5:** StaffProfile retained never deleted; exit interview + service certificate.
  **RBAC (D-#117) — NO new permission:** composes `staff:manage` + `payroll:manage` + `payroll:approve`
  (Principal-only release). **Vocab (app-native, NO wire sync):** EMPLOYMENT_STATUSES += retired/contract_ended;
  OFFBOARDING_TRIGGERS/OFFBOARDING_STATUSES/CLEARANCE_ITEM_STATUSES + BN/EN; verifier §C.11 (no OFFICE-list
  change). 8 new audit kinds; HR firewall extended with `OffboardingCase`. **Gate GREEN (executed):** vocab
  verifier PASS, shared build + shared/server tsc clean, **jest 831/831** (51 suites; 17 new in
  `offboarding.test.ts`; firewall green). **Server-only** (no app — HR precedent; expo export skipped). **Not
  verified live.** Parked (prd-hr §10/H7.7): clearance-list items, BD statutory final-dues timeline (vs D-#29),
  §6 app screens. **HR MODULE COMPLETE (HR-1..HR-5)** — next HR work = the §6/§5 app surfaces + live verification.

  writes `employmentStatus → terminated` (the offboarding TRIGGER; the H6 workflow stays HR-5's court),

  BN/EN + performance:manage/performance:signoff; verifier §C.10 + OFFICE exact-list. 12 new audit kinds

- **Built (Messaging M-7 — staff Expo push, server, D-#116) [branch `worktree-messaging-m7`, PR open
  — coordinator reviews]:** the SEVENTH and FINAL messaging slice per `docs/prd-messaging.md` §5/J-M1 —
  **messaging M-1..M-7 is now COMPLETE**. **VOCAB-FREE** (HR-4 owns `shared/vocab.ts` this cycle):
